## Response:
7/13/2026, 5:19:58 AM

# Cheatsheet - Secrets Management, Key Rotation, JWKS, KMS/Vault, dan Secure Configuration untuk Java/Jersey Microservices

## 1. Tujuan

Cheatsheet ini fokus ke cara mengelola **secret dan key material** untuk authentication/authorization di microservice Java/Jersey.

Masalah yang ingin dicegah:

```text id="af9e2v"
- JWT signing key bocor.
- OAuth client secret hardcoded di repository.
- DB password ada di application.properties.
- API key partner tersimpan plaintext.
- Secret masuk Docker image.
- Secret muncul di log, stack trace, metric label, atau audit payload.
- JWKS cache tidak handle key rotation.
- KMS/Vault down membuat service fail-open.
- Rotation memutus traffic karena tidak ada overlap old/new key.
```

OWASP Secrets Management Cheat Sheet menekankan bahwa proses rotasi biasanya perlu beberapa tahap aman seperti membuat secret baru, memasang secret baru, mengetes secret baru, lalu menyelesaikan rotasi. NIST SP 800-57 Part 1 memberi panduan umum dan best practice untuk pengelolaan cryptographic keying material. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Secrets_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Mental Model

```text id="fnszzd"
Secret:
  value sensitif yang memberi akses.
  contoh: DB password, API key, OAuth client secret.

Key:
  material kriptografis untuk encrypt/decrypt/sign/verify.
  contoh: AES key, RSA private key, HMAC secret.

Certificate:
  public identity binding untuk TLS/mTLS.
  contoh: X.509 cert.

JWKS:
  public key set untuk verifikasi JWT signature.

KMS:
  layanan key management, biasanya untuk encryption key dan envelope encryption.

Vault/Secrets Manager:
  layanan penyimpanan, distribusi, rotation, dynamic secrets, leasing.
```

Rule utama:

```text id="ebecgj"
Source code is not a secret store.
Container image is not a secret store.
GitHub repository is not a secret store.
Logs are not a secret store.
```

---

# 3. Diagram Besar

```mermaid id="pt98m7"
flowchart TD
    A[Developer / CI/CD] --> B[Secret Scanning + Policy]
    B --> C[Build Artifact]
    C --> D[Deploy Runtime]

    E[Vault / Secrets Manager / KMS] --> D
    F[Kubernetes Secret / External Secrets] --> D
    G[Cloud KMS: AWS/Azure/GCP] --> E

    D --> H[Jersey Microservice]
    H --> I[Authentication Filter]
    H --> J[JWT Validator / JWKS Cache]
    H --> K[DB Connection Pool]
    H --> L[Partner HMAC Verifier]

    M[IdP / Authorization Server] --> N[JWKS Endpoint]
    N --> J

    O[Rotation Event] --> E
    O --> N
    O --> P[Invalidate Cache / Reload Config]
    P --> H

    H --> Q[Audit: secret metadata only, no raw secret]
```

---

# 4. Secret Categories

| Category | Contoh | Storage Ideal | Rotation |
|---|---|---|---|
| DB credentials | PostgreSQL username/password | Vault dynamic DB creds / Secret Manager | short TTL or scheduled |
| OAuth client secret | `client_secret` untuk client credentials | Vault/Secret Manager | scheduled + incident |
| JWT signing private key | RSA/EC private key | HSM/KMS/IdP-managed | planned rotation |
| JWT verification public key | JWKS public key | JWKS cache | fetch/refresh |
| HMAC webhook secret | partner/webhook signing secret | Vault/Secret Manager | overlap old/new |
| API key | partner/internal API key | hash + secret store | scheduled + incident |
| TLS private key | server/mTLS cert key | cert manager/HSM/Vault PKI | cert lifecycle |
| Encryption data key | per-secret/per-object key | envelope encryption | generated per encrypt |
| Master/wrapping key | KMS key | cloud KMS/HSM | managed rotation |
| Kubernetes secret | runtime secret material | Kubernetes Secret + encryption at rest/external store | deploy/update |

Kubernetes Secrets dapat dipakai sebagai volume atau environment variable di Pod; Docker Swarm secrets disimpan di encrypted Raft log dan dikirim ke manager melalui mutual TLS. Keduanya membantu runtime injection, tetapi tetap perlu kebijakan akses, encryption, rotation, dan scanning. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/secret/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 5. Apa yang Jangan Dilakukan

```text id="me0vyh"
Jangan:
  - commit secret ke Git
  - taruh secret di Dockerfile ENV
  - bake secret ke image
  - taruh secret plaintext di application.properties
  - log Authorization header
  - log raw JWT/API key/HMAC secret
  - simpan API key plaintext di DB
  - kirim secret via Slack/email/ticket
  - expose secret via /config, /env, /actuator
  - mount secret ke semua container tanpa perlu
```

GitHub push protection dirancang untuk mencegah hardcoded credential seperti secret/token masuk repository sejak proses push, bukan hanya memberi alert setelah secret bocor. ([GitHub Docs](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/secret-security/push-protection?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 6. Secure Configuration Layering

Urutan config yang direkomendasikan:

```text id="f8qo3v"
1. Non-sensitive defaults:
   application.yaml

2. Environment-specific non-secret config:
   config map / deployment variables

3. Secret references:
   secret name/path/key, not raw value

4. Runtime secret provider:
   Vault / AWS Secrets Manager / Azure Key Vault / Kubernetes Secret

5. In-memory use:
   minimal lifetime, never log

6. Rotation/reload:
   refresh connection pool/cache safely
```

Contoh config aman:

```yaml id="ev7z4x"
security:
  jwt:
    issuer: "https://idp.company.com/realms/internal"
    audience: "case-api"
    jwksUri: "https://idp.company.com/realms/internal/protocol/openid-connect/certs"

database:
  credentialsRef: "vault:database/creds/case-api"

partner:
  hmacSecretsRef: "vault:secret/data/partners/acme/hmac"
```

Contoh buruk:

```yaml id="en46je"
database:
  password: "P@ssw0rd123"

oauth:
  clientSecret: "super-secret-client-secret"
```

---

# 7. Environment Variable vs File Mount

| Method | Pros | Cons | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Env var | mudah, umum | bisa muncul di process env/debug dump | cukup untuk low/medium risk, hati-hati |
| Mounted file | bisa permission-scoped, reloadable | perlu filesystem handling | lebih baik untuk cert/key/secret |
| Sidecar/agent inject | dynamic/rotatable | operational complexity | bagus untuk Vault/Kubernetes |
| Direct API call to Vault/KMS | fresh, auditable | runtime dependency | bagus untuk high assurance |
| Build-time injection | sederhana | bocor di artifact | hindari untuk secret |

Untuk Kubernetes, Secret dapat diekspos sebagai env var atau volume; praktik yang lebih aman sering memakai volume/external secret store agar update/rotation dan permission lebih terkendali. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/secret/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 8. Java Secret Access Pattern

Jangan sebar `System.getenv("DB_PASSWORD")` di seluruh codebase.

```java id="lbid4x"
public interface SecretProvider {
    SecretValue getSecret(String ref);
}
```

```java id="kp36ja"
public record SecretValue(
        String ref,
        char[] value,
        Instant loadedAt,
        Instant expiresAt,
        String version
) implements AutoCloseable {

    @Override
    public void close() {
        Arrays.fill(value, '\0');
    }

    public boolean isExpired(Clock clock) {
        return expiresAt != null && Instant.now(clock).isAfter(expiresAt);
    }
}
```

Usage:

```java id="mtcy9w"
try (SecretValue dbPassword = secretProvider.getSecret("vault:database/creds/case-api")) {
    dataSourceFactory.configurePassword(dbPassword.value());
}
```

**Catatan realistis:** di JVM, zeroing `char[]` membantu tetapi tidak sempurna karena library sering butuh `String`. Tetap lebih baik daripada menyebarkan secret sebagai immutable `String` di banyak tempat.

---

# 9. Secret Provider Implementations

```text id="k7hyu8"
com.company.security.secrets
├── SecretProvider.java
├── SecretValue.java
├── EnvironmentSecretProvider.java
├── FileSecretProvider.java
├── VaultSecretProvider.java
├── AwsSecretsManagerProvider.java
├── AzureKeyVaultSecretProvider.java
├── CachingSecretProvider.java
├── RotatingSecretProvider.java
└── SecretRedactor.java
```

Composite provider:

```java id="y3a8u1"
public final class CompositeSecretProvider implements SecretProvider {

    private final Map<String, SecretProvider> providers;

    @Override
    public SecretValue getSecret(String ref) {
        String scheme = ref.substring(0, ref.indexOf(':'));

        SecretProvider provider = providers.get(scheme);

        if (provider == null) {
            throw new SecretConfigurationException("Unsupported secret scheme: " + scheme);
        }

        return provider.getSecret(ref);
    }
}
```

---

# 10. Secret Caching

Cache secret secara hati-hati.

| Secret | Cache? | TTL |
|---|---:|---|
| JWKS public keys | yes | minutes, refresh on unknown `kid` |
| DB dynamic credentials | yes | until lease expiry minus margin |
| HMAC partner secrets | yes | short/medium, version-aware |
| API key hashes | yes | short, invalidated on revoke |
| KMS data keys | no or very short | preferably use SDK/envelope flow |
| OAuth client secret | yes but protected | until rotation/reload |
| Break-glass secret | no | fresh only |

Vault dynamic secrets dan service tokens memiliki lease dengan TTL; saat lease expired, Vault dapat revoke data, sehingga consumer tidak boleh menganggap secret masih valid setelah TTL. ([HashiCorp Developer](https://developer.hashicorp.com/vault/docs/concepts/lease?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 11. CachingSecretProvider

```java id="89ynw1"
public final class CachingSecretProvider implements SecretProvider {

    private final SecretProvider delegate;
    private final SecretCache cache;
    private final Clock clock;

    @Override
    public SecretValue getSecret(String ref) {
        Optional<SecretValue> cached = cache.get(ref)
                .filter(secret -> !secret.isExpired(clock));

        if (cached.isPresent()) {
            return cached.get();
        }

        SecretValue loaded = delegate.getSecret(ref);

        Duration ttl = ttlUntilRefresh(loaded);

        cache.put(ref, loaded, ttl);

        return loaded;
    }

    private Duration ttlUntilRefresh(SecretValue secret) {
        if (secret.expiresAt() == null) {
            return Duration.ofMinutes(5);
        }

        return Duration.between(Instant.now(clock), secret.expiresAt())
                .minus(Duration.ofSeconds(30));
    }
}
```

**Rule:** refresh sebelum expiry, bukan setelah request mulai gagal.

---

# 12. Vault Dynamic Secrets

Vault dynamic DB credentials cocok untuk PostgreSQL jika perusahaan sudah punya Vault maturity.

Flow:

```text id="7bh6mk"
service authenticates to Vault
  -> requests database/creds/case-api
  -> Vault creates temporary DB user/password
  -> returns credentials + lease TTL
  -> service uses credentials
  -> renew lease or let expire
  -> Vault revokes credentials
```

HashiCorp Vault membuat lease untuk dynamic secret dan service-type token; lease membawa TTL dan renewability, dan secret bisa di-revoke saat lease expired. ([HashiCorp Developer](https://developer.hashicorp.com/vault/docs/concepts/lease?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 13. DB Credential Rotation Strategy

## Static DB Secret

```text id="8c31f5"
Secret Manager:
  db/case-api/password

Rotation:
  create new password
  update DB user password
  update secret
  restart/reload app connection pool
```

Risiko:

```text id="8b770i"
Existing connections may continue with old password.
New connections need new password.
Rotation must coordinate pool drain/reconnect.
```

## Dynamic DB Secret

```text id="wlrl1p"
Vault:
  database/creds/case-api

Rotation:
  automatic by lease lifecycle
  app renews/reloads before expiry
```

Caveat:

```text id="5ksrpn"
Connection pool must not keep connections beyond credential lifetime.
Set maxLifetime < secret lease TTL.
```

HikariCP example:

```yaml id="kftgg5"
datasource:
  maximumPoolSize: 20
  maxLifetimeMs: 1500000   # less than credential TTL
  idleTimeoutMs: 300000
```

---

# 14. KMS vs Secret Manager vs Vault

| Tool | Best For | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| KMS | key encryption, envelope encryption, signing/decryption APIs | not usually raw app config store |
| Secret Manager | storing app secrets and versions | DB password, API key, OAuth secret |
| Vault | dynamic secrets, PKI, leasing, secret engines | strong for hybrid/on-prem |
| Kubernetes Secret | runtime injection into pods | protect with RBAC/encryption/external secret |
| ConfigMap | non-sensitive config | never secret |
| HSM | high-assurance private key operations | signing/decryption key protection |

AWS Secrets Manager uses envelope encryption with AWS KMS keys and data keys; when a secret value changes, Secrets Manager requests a new data key from KMS to protect it. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/secretsmanager/latest/userguide/security-encryption.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 15. Envelope Encryption

Envelope encryption pattern:

```text id="tn1fw8"
1. Generate data key.
2. Encrypt data with data key.
3. Encrypt data key with KMS key.
4. Store ciphertext + encrypted data key.
5. On decrypt, KMS decrypts data key.
6. App decrypts data locally or via SDK pattern.
```

Use case:

```text id="m7ek64"
- encrypt sensitive DB column
- encrypt exported file
- encrypt cached secret payload
- encrypt tenant-specific sensitive config
```

AWS KMS documentation describes envelope encryption as protecting data keys with KMS keys managed by KMS. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/developerguide/kms-cryptography.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 16. Key Hierarchy

```text id="lr0pma"
Root key / KMS key:
  stored and protected by KMS/HSM

Key-encryption key:
  wraps data keys

Data key:
  encrypts actual data

Application secret:
  DB password/API key/HMAC secret

Public verification key:
  JWKS RSA/EC public key

Private signing key:
  used by IdP/signing service, should not be in normal app service
```

Rule:

```text id="ylsveo"
Normal resource server should need JWT public keys, not JWT private signing keys.
```

---

# 17. JWT Signing Key vs Verification Key

| Key | Who Has It | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| Private signing key | IdP/Auth server only | sign JWT |
| Public verification key | resource servers | verify JWT |
| HMAC shared signing key | issuer + verifier | sign/verify symmetric JWT |
| JWKS | public key set | publish public keys |

For microservice API:

```text id="d4pcna"
case-api:
  needs JWKS public keys

case-api:
  should not have IdP private signing key
```

Avoid symmetric JWT signing (`HS256`) across many microservices unless you intentionally accept that every verifier with the shared secret can also sign tokens.

---

# 18. JWKS Cache and Rotation

JWKS rotation safe pattern:

```text id="a8fy3r"
1. IdP publishes old + new public key in JWKS.
2. IdP starts signing new tokens with new private key.
3. Resource servers fetch JWKS and accept both keys.
4. Wait until old tokens expire.
5. Remove old key from JWKS.
```

Resource server behavior:

```text id="qlbbmj"
- cache JWKS for TTL
- on unknown kid, refresh JWKS once
- if still unknown kid, reject token
- never fetch JWKS from token-controlled URL
- only use configured trusted jwks_uri
- fail closed if key cannot be resolved after safe cache window
```

OpenID Connect uses discovery and JWKS-style key distribution for clients/resource servers to obtain signing keys from trusted issuer metadata; the issuer/JWKS URI must be configured/trusted, not chosen by the token. ([OpenID Foundation](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 19. JWKS Key Resolver

```java id="z9qoyv"
public final class JwksKeyResolver {

    private final JwksClient jwksClient;
    private final JwksCache cache;
    private final URI trustedJwksUri;

    public PublicKey resolve(String issuer, String kid, String alg) {
        Jwks jwks = cache.get(issuer)
                .orElseGet(() -> refresh(issuer));

        Optional<PublicKey> key = jwks.findKey(kid, alg);

        if (key.isPresent()) {
            return key.get();
        }

        // unknown kid: refresh once
        Jwks refreshed = refresh(issuer);

        return refreshed.findKey(kid, alg)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new InvalidAccessTokenException("unknown_key_id"));
    }

    private Jwks refresh(String issuer) {
        if (!trustedIssuerRegistry.isTrusted(issuer, trustedJwksUri)) {
            throw new InvalidAccessTokenException("untrusted_issuer");
        }

        Jwks jwks = jwksClient.fetch(trustedJwksUri);
        cache.put(issuer, jwks, Duration.ofMinutes(10));
        return jwks;
    }
}
```

---

# 20. JWKS Cache Failure Modes

| Failure | Safe Behavior |
|---|---|
| JWKS endpoint down, cached key still valid | use cached key until TTL/grace policy |
| JWKS endpoint down, unknown `kid` | reject token |
| JWKS returns key with disallowed alg | reject |
| Token uses `alg=none` | reject |
| Key type mismatch | reject |
| Issuer mismatch | reject |
| JWKS URL from token claim | ignore/reject |
| Old key removed too early | valid old tokens fail; operational incident |
| New key not published before signing | all new tokens fail |

---

# 21. Algorithm Allowlist

Never trust JWT `alg` blindly.

```yaml id="q4haz0"
security:
  jwt:
    allowedAlgorithms:
      - RS256
      - ES256
```

Validation:

```java id="eb1mlu"
if (!config.allowedAlgorithms().contains(header.algorithm())) {
    throw new InvalidAccessTokenException("disallowed_jwt_algorithm");
}
```

Rule:

```text id="879zd5"
Header selects among allowed verification methods.
Header does not define trust policy.
```

---

# 22. Key Rotation Types

| Rotation Type | Example | Strategy |
|---|---|---|
| Planned rotation | regular JWT signing key rotation | overlap old/new |
| Emergency rotation | private key leaked | revoke old immediately, shorten acceptance |
| Secret rotation | DB password/API key | dual-read/dual-write where needed |
| HMAC secret rotation | partner signature secret | accept old+new during window |
| TLS cert renewal | server/mTLS cert | cert-manager/PKI automation |
| KMS key rotation | master/wrapping key | managed by KMS |
| Data re-encryption | encrypted column/file | background rewrap/re-encrypt |

AWS KMS supports optional automatic and on-demand rotation for customer managed keys, while Azure Key Vault supports configuring automated key rotation policies. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/developerguide/rotate-keys.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 23. Rotation State Machine

```mermaid id="xzbl4p"
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Active
    Active --> Rotating: create new version
    Rotating --> DualAccept: old + new accepted
    DualAccept --> NewPrimary: new used for signing/encryption
    NewPrimary --> OldDeprecated: old accepted only for verification/decrypt
    OldDeprecated --> Retired: old tokens/ciphertexts expired
    Retired --> Destroyed: after retention policy
```

For signing keys:

```text id="67adsw"
Active:
  old key signs + verifies

DualAccept:
  new public key published
  old still signs or verifies

NewPrimary:
  new key signs
  old verifies

Retired:
  old no longer needed for active tokens

Destroyed:
  private old key destroyed after retention/legal policy
```

---

# 24. HMAC/API Key Rotation with Overlap

Database:

```sql id="56xc3l"
CREATE TABLE api_client_secret (
    id              UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    client_id       TEXT NOT NULL,
    secret_hash     TEXT NOT NULL,
    secret_prefix   TEXT NOT NULL,
    version         INT NOT NULL,
    active          BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    primary_secret  BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
    not_before      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    expires_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
    revoked_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Verification:

```text id="2qxj33"
accept secret if:
  active = true
  not_before <= now
  revoked_at is null
  expires_at is null or expires_at > now
  hash matches
```

Rotation:

```text id="voe4ee"
1. Create v2 secret.
2. Mark v2 primary.
3. Partner starts signing with v2.
4. Server accepts v1 + v2.
5. After overlap, revoke v1.
6. Audit rotation completion.
```

---

# 25. OAuth Client Secret Rotation

For client credentials:

```text id="oknozv"
1. Register new client secret in IdP.
2. Store new secret in Vault/Secret Manager.
3. Deploy/reload calling service to use new secret.
4. Keep old secret active during overlap.
5. Verify token acquisition with new secret.
6. Revoke old secret.
7. Audit rotation.
```

If IdP supports multiple active secrets per client, use overlap. If not, plan coordinated deployment window.

---

# 26. Kubernetes Secrets

Kubernetes Secret example:

```yaml id="3erbgz"
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: case-api-secrets
type: Opaque
stringData:
  database-password: "REDACTED"
```

Mount as file:

```yaml id="n9wo7l"
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: case-api
spec:
  template:
    spec:
      containers:
        - name: case-api
          image: registry.company.com/case-api:1.0.0
          volumeMounts:
            - name: secrets
              mountPath: /var/run/secrets/case-api
              readOnly: true
      volumes:
        - name: secrets
          secret:
            secretName: case-api-secrets
```

Kubernetes docs note Secrets can be mounted as volumes or exposed as environment variables; avoid exposing secrets to Pods that do not need them. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/secret/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 27. Kubernetes Secret Hardening

```text id="uea6r1"
[ ] Enable encryption at rest for Kubernetes Secrets.
[ ] Restrict RBAC: only needed service accounts can read specific secret.
[ ] Prefer mounted files over env vars for high-risk secrets.
[ ] Do not mount all secrets into every pod.
[ ] Use External Secrets / CSI driver / Vault agent if available.
[ ] Rotate secrets and restart/reload pods safely.
[ ] Disable kubectl access to secrets for broad developer roles.
[ ] Avoid printing env/config in logs.
[ ] Watch for secret exposure in crash dumps.
```

---

# 28. Docker Secrets

Docker Swarm secrets are delivered to services and stored encrypted in the Raft log according to Docker docs. This helps in Swarm mode, but Docker Compose/local development needs separate handling and must not bake secrets into images. ([Docker Documentation](https://docs.docker.com/engine/swarm/secrets/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Dockerfile anti-pattern:

```dockerfile id="l5gmpu"
ENV DB_PASSWORD=super-secret
```

Better runtime pattern:

```text id="miq6t2"
- pass secret at runtime
- mount secret file
- use orchestrator secret feature
- use Vault/Secret Manager agent
```

---

# 29. GitHub / CI/CD Secret Guardrails

Minimum guardrails:

```text id="xenrt8"
- GitHub secret scanning / push protection
- pre-commit secret scanning
- CI secret scan
- container image secret scan
- dependency/config scan
- block printing environment variables
- mask CI secrets
- least-privilege GitHub Actions secrets
- environment protection rules for prod secrets
```

GitHub push protection blocks supported secrets before they are pushed and creates alerts if bypassed. ([GitHub Docs](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/secret-security/push-protection?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 30. Secret Redaction

Central redactor:

```java id="z34j6s"
public final class SecretRedactor {

    private static final Pattern BEARER =
            Pattern.compile("Bearer\\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/=-]+", Pattern.CASE_INSENSITIVE);

    private static final Pattern API_KEY =
            Pattern.compile("(api[_-]?key\\s*[:=]\\s*)([^\\s,}]+)", Pattern.CASE_INSENSITIVE);

    private SecretRedactor() {}

    public static String redact(String input) {
        if (input == null) {
            return null;
        }

        String value = BEARER.matcher(input).replaceAll("Bearer [REDACTED]");
        value = API_KEY.matcher(value).replaceAll("$1[REDACTED]");

        return value;
    }
}
```

Logging wrapper:

```java id="dedbc5"
LOG.warn(
        "Authentication failed reason={} request={} correlationId={}",
        reason,
        SecretRedactor.redact(requestSummary),
        correlationId
);
```

Better: avoid constructing strings containing secrets in the first place.

---

# 31. Log and Audit Rules

Do log:

```text id="fu1ohl"
- secret reference/path, if not sensitive
- secret version
- key id / kid
- certificate thumbprint
- rotation event id
- client id
- reason code
- correlation id
```

Do not log:

```text id="cdaxrr"
- raw token
- raw API key
- raw HMAC secret
- DB password
- private key
- OAuth client secret
- full Authorization header
- full environment dump
- decrypted secret value
```

Example safe audit:

```json id="uaz4cj"
{
  "eventType": "SECRET_ROTATED",
  "secretRef": "vault:secret/data/partners/acme/hmac",
  "secretVersion": "v3",
  "clientId": "partner-acme",
  "rotatedBy": "platform-admin-1",
  "correlationId": "req-123"
}
```

---

# 32. Secure Config in Maven/Java

Never put secrets in:

```text id="xfpqwm"
- pom.xml
- settings.xml committed to repo
- application.properties committed to repo
- test resources committed to repo
- Dockerfile
- Helm values committed plaintext
```

For Maven private repository credentials, use local/user-level `settings.xml` or CI secret injection, not committed project config.

Example safe placeholder:

```xml id="ph02yc"
<server>
  <id>company-artifacts</id>
  <username>${env.MAVEN_REPO_USERNAME}</username>
  <password>${env.MAVEN_REPO_PASSWORD}</password>
</server>
```

But in CI, ensure env vars are masked and not echoed.

---

# 33. Spring-less Jersey Config Pattern

If using Jersey without Spring Boot, keep config loading explicit.

```java id="m3s83s"
public record SecurityConfig(
        URI issuer,
        URI jwksUri,
        Set<String> audiences,
        Set<String> allowedAlgorithms,
        String databaseCredentialRef,
        String partnerSecretRef
) {}
```

Loader:

```java id="jbk27c"
public final class SecurityConfigLoader {

    private final ConfigSource configSource;

    public SecurityConfig load() {
        return new SecurityConfig(
                URI.create(required("SECURITY_JWT_ISSUER")),
                URI.create(required("SECURITY_JWT_JWKS_URI")),
                Set.of(required("SECURITY_JWT_AUDIENCE")),
                Set.of("RS256"),
                required("DATABASE_CREDENTIAL_REF"),
                required("PARTNER_SECRET_REF")
        );
    }

    private String required(String key) {
        String value = configSource.get(key);

        if (value == null || value.isBlank()) {
            throw new ConfigurationException("Missing required config: " + key);
        }

        return value;
    }
}
```

---

# 34. Secret References, Not Secret Values

```text id="xw4axu"
DATABASE_CREDENTIAL_REF=vault:database/creds/case-api
PARTNER_ACME_HMAC_SECRET_REF=vault:secret/data/partners/acme/hmac
JWT_JWKS_URI=https://idp.company.com/realms/internal/protocol/openid-connect/certs
```

Application startup:

```text id="x6bgph"
config contains ref
  -> SecretProvider resolves ref
  -> secret loaded into runtime
  -> value not printed
```

---

# 35. Fail-Closed vs Fail-Open

| Secret Dependency | Failure | Safe Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| JWKS refresh | endpoint down, known key cached | use valid cached key within TTL/grace |
| JWKS unknown `kid` | cannot fetch key | reject token |
| Vault DB secret | cannot renew | stop creating new DB connections before expiry |
| HMAC secret store | cannot load partner secret | reject request |
| KMS decrypt | decrypt fails | deny/fail operation |
| Secret cache corrupt | invalid value | reload; if fail, reject |
| Rotation event missed | old secret used | version/TTL fallback |

Rule:

```text id="ze77ug"
Do not convert secret/key management failure into authorization allow.
```

---

# 36. Startup vs Runtime Secret Loading

| Pattern | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| Startup load | fail fast | rotation requires reload/restart |
| Runtime lazy load | flexible | request latency, dependency outage |
| Sidecar file update | decoupled | app must watch/reload |
| Agent template render | mature with Vault | operational complexity |
| SDK with cache | integrated | app logic more complex |

Recommended hybrid:

```text id="je02ry"
- fail fast for mandatory config
- cache with TTL for runtime secrets
- support reload for rotated secrets
- use old+new overlap where possible
- health/readiness reflects unrecoverable secret failure
```

---

# 37. Secret Reload

For file-mounted secret:

```java id="gpur68"
public final class FileSecretReloader {

    private final Path path;
    private volatile SecretValue current;

    public SecretValue current() {
        SecretValue local = current;

        if (local == null || local.isExpired(Clock.systemUTC())) {
            current = loadFromFile(path);
        }

        return current;
    }

    private SecretValue loadFromFile(Path path) {
        try {
            char[] value = Files.readString(path).toCharArray();

            return new SecretValue(
                    path.toString(),
                    value,
                    Instant.now(),
                    Instant.now().plus(Duration.ofMinutes(5)),
                    fileVersion(path)
            );
        } catch (IOException e) {
            throw new SecretLoadException("Cannot load secret file: " + path, e);
        }
    }
}
```

---

# 38. Certificate and mTLS Rotation

mTLS cert rotation pattern:

```text id="pw7xrr"
1. Issue new cert before old expires.
2. Trust bundle includes old + new CA/cert as needed.
3. Workload reloads cert without downtime if possible.
4. Peers accept both during overlap.
5. Remove old cert after expiry.
```

Important:

```text id="fj68j0"
- monitor cert expiry
- alert before expiry
- do not store private key in repo/image
- restrict file permission
- use cert-manager/Vault PKI/service mesh where possible
```

---

# 39. Secret Incident Response

If secret leaks:

```text id="tjbkp4"
1. Identify secret type and scope.
2. Revoke/disable leaked secret immediately.
3. Rotate replacement secret/key.
4. Invalidate sessions/tokens if needed.
5. Search logs/repos/images/artifacts for exposure.
6. Audit usage of leaked secret.
7. Review blast radius.
8. Patch root cause.
9. Add detection/prevention rule.
10. Document incident timeline.
```

For private signing key leak:

```text id="f7dml0"
- stop using leaked key
- remove old key from signing
- publish new JWKS
- consider invalidating issued tokens
- shorten token lifetime temporarily
- audit suspicious token usage
```

---

# 40. Secret Rotation Checklist

```text id="i4bqw7"
[ ] New secret/key created.
[ ] New version stored in secret manager.
[ ] Consumers can read new version.
[ ] Old and new accepted during overlap if needed.
[ ] New version becomes primary.
[ ] Metrics confirm new version used.
[ ] Old version revoked after safe window.
[ ] Cache invalidated/reloaded.
[ ] Audit event emitted.
[ ] Rollback plan exists.
```

---

# 41. JWKS Rotation Checklist

```text id="q9biqb"
[ ] Generate new signing key securely.
[ ] Publish new public key in JWKS before use.
[ ] Keep old public key in JWKS.
[ ] Start signing with new private key.
[ ] Resource servers refresh JWKS.
[ ] Monitor unknown kid / invalid signature spike.
[ ] Wait until old token max lifetime expires.
[ ] Retire old key from JWKS.
[ ] Destroy/archive old private key per policy.
[ ] Audit rotation.
```

---

# 42. PostgreSQL Secrets

For DB access:

```text id="6g1ia0"
Recommended:
  - least-privilege DB role per service
  - no shared superuser account
  - separate migration user vs runtime user
  - dynamic credentials if possible
  - connection pool maxLifetime < credential TTL
  - rotate password safely
  - no DB URL with password in logs
```

Bad:

```text id="vbwnub"
jdbc:postgresql://db:5432/app?user=postgres&password=supersecret
```

Better:

```text id="31ifk3"
jdbc URL without password logged
password loaded separately from SecretProvider
```

---

# 43. MyBatis/JDBC Logging Safety

Disable SQL parameter logging for secrets.

Risk:

```text id="dpki7j"
INSERT INTO api_client_secret(secret_hash, ...)
UPDATE oauth_client SET client_secret = ?
```

Even if prepared statement hides values, some loggers/interceptors may print bound parameters.

Checklist:

```text id="n0ber3"
[ ] MyBatis logging does not print secret values.
[ ] JDBC URL password redacted.
[ ] Exception mapper does not expose SQL.
[ ] Audit logs secret metadata only.
[ ] DB migration logs do not print inserted secret.
```

---

# 44. Secret Access Authorization

Not every service should read every secret.

Policy:

```text id="zhcm4f"
case-api:
  can read:
    database/creds/case-api
    secret/data/case-api/oauth-client
    secret/data/partners/acme/hmac

  cannot read:
    database/creds/admin
    secret/data/idp/signing-private-key
    secret/data/other-service/*
```

Vault/KMS/IAM policy should enforce this outside the app.

---

# 45. KMS IAM / Access Policy

General rules:

```text id="sox89n"
- allow decrypt only for needed service identity
- separate encrypt/decrypt/sign permissions
- separate prod/staging keys
- no broad wildcard decrypt
- audit every decrypt/sign operation if possible
- alert on unusual decrypt volume
- rotate keys per policy
```

For AWS/Azure/GCP/on-prem KMS, model access by workload identity/service account, not human shared credentials.

---

# 46. Multi-Cloud / On-Prem Strategy

| Environment | Recommended |
|---|---|
| AWS | AWS Secrets Manager + KMS + IAM role |
| Azure | Azure Key Vault + managed identity/workload identity |
| Kubernetes on-prem | Vault + Kubernetes auth + CSI/agent |
| VM on-prem | Vault agent + mTLS/cert auth |
| Hybrid | common secret abstraction + provider-specific policy |
| Local dev | dev-only secrets, never production secrets |

Azure Key Vault supports automated key rotation configuration, and AWS KMS supports automatic/on-demand rotation for customer-managed keys; exact capabilities differ by key type and provider. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/key-vault/keys/how-to-configure-key-rotation?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 47. Metrics and Alerts

```text id="l38wtb"
secret_load_success_total{provider, secret_ref}
secret_load_failure_total{provider, reason}
secret_cache_hit_total{provider}
secret_cache_miss_total{provider}
secret_rotation_total{secret_type}
secret_version_active{secret_ref, version}
jwks_refresh_success_total{issuer}
jwks_refresh_failure_total{issuer, reason}
jwt_unknown_kid_total{issuer}
jwt_invalid_signature_total{issuer}
kms_decrypt_failure_total{key_id}
vault_lease_renew_failure_total{secret_ref}
cert_expiry_days{service, cert}
secret_redaction_match_total
```

Alerts:

```text id="3hpujx"
- cert expires in < 14 days
- JWKS refresh failure sustained
- unknown kid spike
- invalid signature spike after rotation
- Vault lease renewal failure
- KMS decrypt failure spike
- secret load failure in production
- old secret version still used after rotation window
```

---

# 48. Audit Events

## Secret Loaded

{
  "eventType": "SECRET_LOADED",
  "secretRef": "vault:database/creds/case-api",
  "secretVersion": "lease-123",

}

{

}

---

# 49. Testing Matrix

```text id="87sb1e"
[ ] App fails startup if mandatory secret ref missing.
[ ] Secret value never appears in logs.
[ ] Secret value never appears in error response.
[ ] API key is stored hashed, not plaintext.
[ ] HMAC verifier supports old+new secret during rotation.
[ ] Old HMAC secret rejected after revoke.
[ ] JWKS resolver uses cached key.
[ ] Unknown kid triggers one JWKS refresh.
[ ] Unknown kid after refresh rejects token.
[ ] Disallowed JWT alg rejected.
[ ] JWKS URL from token is ignored.
[ ] Vault lease expiry causes reload/renew.
[ ] DB pool maxLifetime less than dynamic credential TTL.
[ ] Secret provider failure denies sensitive operation.
[ ] Secret scanning catches committed fake secret.
[ ] Kubernetes pod cannot read unrelated secret.
[ ] MyBatis/JDBC logs do not print password/secret.
[ ] Rotation emits audit event.
```

---

# 50. Example Test - Secret Redaction

```java id="pbydyk"
@Test
void shouldRedactBearerTokenFromLogs() {
    String input = "Authorization: Bearer eyJhbGciOiJSUzI1NiJ9.secret.payload";

    String redacted = SecretRedactor.redact(input);

    assertFalse(redacted.contains("eyJhbGciOiJSUzI1NiJ9"));
    assertTrue(redacted.contains("Bearer [REDACTED]"));
}
```

---

# 51. Example Test - JWKS Unknown Kid Refresh Once

```java id="jaeqgq"
@Test
void unknownKidShouldRefreshJwksOnceThenReject() {
    jwksClient.stubInitialKeys(List.of(key("old-kid")));
    jwksClient.stubRefreshedKeys(List.of(key("old-kid")));

    assertThrows(
            InvalidAccessTokenException.class,
            () -> keyResolver.resolve(
                    "https://idp.company.com/realms/internal",
                    "new-kid",
                    "RS256"
            )
    );

    assertEquals(1, jwksClient.refreshCount());
}
```

---

# 52. Example Test - HMAC Secret Rotation

```java id="yy9phg"
@Test
void shouldAcceptOldAndNewHmacSecretsDuringRotationWindow() {
    partnerSecretStore.addActiveSecret("partner-acme", "v1", oldSecret);
    partnerSecretStore.addActiveSecret("partner-acme", "v2", newSecret);

    SignedRequest oldSigned = signer.signWithSecret(oldSecret, request);
    SignedRequest newSigned = signer.signWithSecret(newSecret, request);

    assertTrue(verifier.verify("partner-acme", oldSigned));
    assertTrue(verifier.verify("partner-acme", newSigned));
}
```

```java id="ow6nfa"
@Test
void shouldRejectOldHmacSecretAfterRevocation() {
    partnerSecretStore.revoke("partner-acme", "v1");

    SignedRequest oldSigned = signer.signWithSecret(oldSecret, request);

    assertFalse(verifier.verify("partner-acme", oldSigned));
}
```

---

# 53. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Risiko | Perbaikan |
|---|---|---|
| Secret committed to repo | credential leak | secret scanning + secret manager |
| Secret baked into Docker image | image leak = secret leak | runtime injection |
| Raw token/API key logged | credential reuse | redaction + never log |
| JWT private key in resource server | token forgery blast radius | only IdP holds private key |
| JWKS cache never refreshes | key rotation failure | TTL + unknown kid refresh |
| JWKS URL from token trusted | SSRF/key confusion | configured trusted URI |
| HMAC rotation no overlap | partner downtime | old+new accept window |
| Secret rotation no audit | poor traceability | rotation audit |
| Env dump endpoint enabled | massive leak | disable/sanitize |
| DB superuser shared by app | huge blast radius | least-privilege DB role |
| Vault/KMS outage fail-open | unauthorized access | fail-closed |
| Secret in metric label | long-term leak | metadata only |

---

# 54. Code Review Checklist

```text id="vd4uwq"
[ ] No raw secret in code/config/test resources.
[ ] No secret in Dockerfile/image layer.
[ ] Secret references used instead of secret values.
[ ] Secret provider abstraction exists.
[ ] Logs/error responses redact credentials.
[ ] JWT private signing keys are not in resource services.
[ ] JWKS uses trusted configured jwks_uri.
[ ] JWKS unknown kid refresh behavior is implemented.
[ ] JWT alg allowlist exists.
[ ] API keys are hashed, not plaintext.
[ ] HMAC/API key rotation supports overlap.
[ ] DB credential rotation plan exists.
[ ] Connection pool lifetime respects dynamic credential TTL.
[ ] Kubernetes RBAC restricts secret read access.
[ ] CI secret scanning/push protection enabled.
[ ] Secret rotation emits audit and metrics.
[ ] KMS/Vault failure behavior is fail-closed for sensitive operations.
```

---

# 55. Minimal Production Invariants

```text id="p1nfnr"
1. Secrets never live in source code or container images.
2. Secret values never appear in logs, metrics, audit payloads, or error responses.
3. Resource servers verify JWT with public keys, not private signing keys.
4. JWKS is fetched only from trusted configured issuer metadata/JWKS URI.
5. Unknown JWT kid refreshes JWKS once, then fails closed.
6. API keys/secrets are stored hashed or protected by secret manager.
7. Rotation supports old/new overlap where clients need migration.
8. Dynamic secrets must be refreshed before lease expiry.
9. Secret access is least-privilege by service identity.
10. Secret/key rotation must be observable, audited, and tested.
```

---

# 56. Roadmap Singkat

| Urutan | Fokus | Target |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Secret inventory | Tahu semua DB/API/OAuth/JWT/HMAC/cert secrets |
| 2 | Secret storage | Pindah dari repo/config ke secret manager |
| 3 | Runtime injection | Kubernetes/Vault/KMS integration |
| 4 | Redaction | Token/secret tidak muncul di log/error |
| 5 | JWKS handling | Cache, unknown kid refresh, alg allowlist |
| 6 | Rotation | Old/new overlap dan audit |
| 7 | Dynamic secrets | Vault DB creds/lease-aware pool |
| 8 | KMS/envelope encryption | Data/key hierarchy aman |
| 9 | CI/CD guardrails | secret scanning, image scanning |
| 10 | Tests/alerts | Rotation dan secret failure tidak regresi |

## Response:
7/13/2026, 5:23:37 AM

# Cheatsheet - Threat Modeling Authentication/Authorization di Jersey/JAX-RS Microservices

## 1. Tujuan

Threat modeling untuk authentication/authorization membantu kita menjawab:

```text id="utk3hz"
Apa asset yang dilindungi?
Siapa actor yang bisa menyerang?
Trust boundary ada di mana?
Data/identity/permission mengalir lewat mana?
Apa yang bisa dimanipulasi?
Apa dampaknya?
Control apa yang mencegah, mendeteksi, dan membatasi dampak?
Test apa yang membuktikan control itu bekerja?
```

OWASP Threat Modeling Cheat Sheet memosisikan threat modeling sebagai praktik untuk mengidentifikasi, mengkomunikasikan, dan memahami threat serta mitigation dalam konteks sesuatu yang bernilai; Microsoft Threat Modeling Tool memakai STRIDE untuk membantu mengidentifikasi threat dan mitigation. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Threat_Modeling_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Scope Sistem

Konteks stack:

```text id="ik1te4"
Client / Browser / Partner / Service
  -> API Gateway / Ingress / Nginx
  -> Jersey/JAX-RS ContainerRequestFilter
  -> Authentication
  -> SecurityContext / Principal
  -> Authorization Filter
  -> Application Service Policy
  -> MyBatis Mapper
  -> PostgreSQL
  -> Audit / Observability
```

Threat modeling di sini fokus ke:

```text id="nsdw8u"
- authentication
- identity
- token validation
- service-to-service authentication
- authorization
- permission
- role
- ACL
- object-level authorization
- tenant isolation
- delegation
- impersonation
- audit
- replay protection
- secret/key management
```

---

# 3. Diagram Threat Surface

```mermaid id="qsgwdn"
flowchart TD
    A[External User / Browser] --> B[Gateway / Ingress]
    C[Partner System] --> B
    D[Internal Service] --> B
    E[Attacker / Compromised Client] --> B

    B --> F[Jersey Authentication Filter]
    F --> G[Token / API Key / HMAC / mTLS Validation]
    G --> H[SecurityContext + SecurityActor]

    H --> I[Jersey Authorization Filter]
    I --> J[Application Service Policy]
    J --> K[Object-Level Authorization]
    K --> L[Tenant / Workflow / State Guard]
    L --> M[MyBatis Mapper]
    M --> N[(PostgreSQL)]

    O[IdP / Authorization Server] --> G
    P[JWKS Endpoint] --> G
    Q[Entitlement DB / PDP / ACL Store] --> J
    R[Secrets Manager / KMS / Vault] --> G

    F --> S[Audit]
    I --> S
    J --> S
    K --> S
    M --> S

    E -. attacks .-> F
    E -. attacks .-> I
    E -. attacks .-> J
    E -. attacks .-> M
```

---

# 4. Threat Modeling Method

Pakai 4 pertanyaan sederhana:

| Pertanyaan | Untuk AuthN/AuthZ |
|---|---|
| Apa yang sedang dibangun? | Diagram request flow, token flow, permission flow, tenant flow |
| Apa yang bisa salah? | STRIDE, abuse case, attack tree |
| Apa mitigasinya? | Filter, policy, SQL guard, audit, cache invalidation |
| Apakah mitigasi bekerja? | Unit/integration/security regression tests |

OWASP Threat Modeling Cheat Sheet memberi pendekatan praktis untuk threat modeling sebagai aktivitas desain dan engineering, bukan hanya dokumen compliance. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Threat_Modeling_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 5. STRIDE untuk Authentication/Authorization

STRIDE mengelompokkan threat ke: **Spoofing, Tampering, Repudiation, Information Disclosure, Denial of Service, Elevation of Privilege**. Microsoft Threat Modeling Tool memakai STRIDE per element untuk membantu guided analysis threat dan mitigation. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/security/develop/threat-modeling-tool?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

| STRIDE | Di AuthN/AuthZ Artinya | Contoh |
|---|---|---|
| Spoofing | Menyamar sebagai user/service/partner lain | fake token, stolen API key, spoofed mTLS header |
| Tampering | Mengubah token, request, object ID, tenant ID, permission | JWT alg confusion, body tampering, path ID tampering |
| Repudiation | Actor menyangkal action | audit hilang, impersonation overwrite actor |
| Information Disclosure | Data/security detail bocor | cross-tenant 403 leak, token logged, stack trace |
| Denial of Service | Auth service/cache/PDP dibuat down | JWKS flood, PDP timeout, expensive permission query |
| Elevation of Privilege | Privilege naik tanpa hak | role bypass, confused deputy, BOLA, admin endpoint exposed |

---

# 6. Asset yang Dilindungi

| Asset | Kenapa Penting | Threat Utama |
|---|---|---|
| Access token | Bukti akses API | theft, replay, token substitution |
| Refresh token | Bisa mendapatkan token baru | theft, long-lived abuse |
| API key/HMAC secret | Partner/internal identity | leakage, replay |
| JWT signing key | Bisa forge token | catastrophic compromise |
| JWKS public key trust | Token verification | key confusion, malicious JWKS |
| SecurityContext | Identity internal | principal spoofing/mapping bug |
| Permission catalog | Capability source of truth | unauthorized grant, stale role |
| ACL grants | Object access | stale/expired grant, wrong subject |
| Tenant boundary | Data isolation | cross-tenant leak |
| Audit log | Forensic evidence | deletion, missing actor chain |
| PostgreSQL data | Business/customer data | query missing tenant predicate |
| Camunda task/process | Workflow state | unauthorized complete/escalate |

---

# 7. Trust Boundaries

```mermaid id="wlcw9i"
flowchart LR
    A[Untrusted Internet] -->|HTTPS| B[Gateway]
    B -->|Trusted forwarding? limited| C[Jersey API]
    C -->|Validated token only| D[SecurityContext]
    D --> E[Application Policy]
    E --> F[MyBatis SQL]
    F --> G[(PostgreSQL)]

    H[IdP/JWKS] -->|Trusted configured issuer| C
    I[Secrets Manager] -->|Service identity auth| C
    J[PDP/Entitlement] -->|Service-to-service auth| E
```

Trust boundary penting:

| Boundary | Jangan Percaya | Harus Validasi |
|---|---|---|
| Client → Gateway | header, token, object ID, tenant ID | TLS, auth, rate limit |
| Gateway → Service | spoofed forwarding headers | trusted proxy config, strip headers |
| Token → Principal | raw claims | signature, issuer, audience, expiry |
| Resource Method → Service | user input | object/tenant/state policy |
| Service → DB | mapper parameters | tenant/status/version guard |
| Service → PDP | PDP response | timeout/fail-closed/contract |
| App → Audit | log payload | redaction, correlation ID |

---

# 8. Threat Catalog - Authentication

| Threat | STRIDE | Attack | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Token substitution | Spoofing | Token untuk API A dipakai ke API B | validate `aud` |
| Fake issuer | Spoofing | Token dari issuer tidak trusted | exact issuer allowlist |
| JWT alg confusion | Tampering/EoP | attacker ubah alg/header | algorithm allowlist |
| Expired token accepted | EoP | `exp` tidak dicek | validate exp/nbf/iat |
| ID token accepted as access token | EoP | client kirim ID token ke API | token type/audience check |
| Stolen bearer token replay | Spoofing | token dipakai ulang | short TTL, mTLS/cert-bound, introspection |
| Unknown `kid` abuse | DoS/Tampering | force JWKS refresh terus | refresh once, cache, rate limit |
| Raw token logged | Info Disclosure | log bocor | redaction/no token logging |
| Missing tenant claim | EoP | request masuk tanpa tenant | deny tenant-scoped endpoints |
| Token claims trusted before signature | Tampering | parse claims dulu | verify before trusting claims |

OWASP API Security 2023 menempatkan Broken Authentication sebagai API2 dan Broken Object Level Authorization sebagai API1; untuk BOLA, OWASP menjelaskan attacker dapat memanipulasi object ID di path, query, header, atau payload. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa2-broken-authentication/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 9. Threat Catalog - Authorization

| Threat | STRIDE | Attack | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| BOLA/IDOR | EoP/Info Disclosure | ubah `/cases/{id}` ke case orang lain | object-level auth setiap access |
| Broken function-level auth | EoP | user biasa call admin endpoint | endpoint classification + permission |
| Missing tenant predicate | Info Disclosure/EoP | query return tenant lain | `tenant_id` di mapper/SQL |
| Role bypass | EoP | role `ADMIN` terlalu luas | permission + policy |
| ACL stale | EoP | revoked grant masih cached | versioned cache + TTL |
| Confused deputy | EoP | service privileged dipakai user lemah | authorize service + original user |
| Impersonation abuse | Repudiation/EoP | support jadi user tanpa grant | grant, expiry, audit actor chain |
| UI-only authorization | EoP | call API langsung | backend enforcement |
| Bulk operation bypass | EoP | one allow updates many objects | per-object auth |
| Search/count leakage | Info Disclosure | total count bocor | same visibility criteria |

OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet menekankan least privilege, deny by default, dan validasi permission pada setiap request; ini sangat relevan untuk mencegah authorization bypass dan privilege escalation. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 10. Threat Catalog - Service-to-Service

| Threat | STRIDE | Attack | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| User token calls internal endpoint | EoP | user pakai token ke `/internal/*` | require `ActorType.SERVICE` |
| Wrong audience service token | Spoofing | token untuk service lain | validate audience |
| Spoofed mTLS header | Spoofing | client kirim `X-MTLS-Verified:true` | strip at gateway, trust only proxy |
| Service allowed all tenants | EoP | compromised service reads all tenants | allowed tenant/resource scope |
| Static service secret leaked | Spoofing | client secret reused | secret manager, rotation, mTLS |
| Internal endpoint public | Info Disclosure/EoP | `/internal/*` reachable internet | gateway block + app auth |
| Service permission too broad | EoP | `internal:*` | granular internal permissions |
| Replay internal command | Tampering/DoS | repeat mutation | nonce/idempotency |
| Missing service audit | Repudiation | cannot trace caller | service authz audit |

---

# 11. Threat Catalog - API Key / HMAC / Webhook

| Threat | STRIDE | Attack | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| API key leak | Spoofing | stolen key reused | hash storage, rotation, scopes |
| Signature over parsed JSON | Tampering | raw body changed but parsed same-ish | sign raw body digest |
| No timestamp | Replay | old request replayed | timestamp tolerance |
| Timestamp no nonce | Replay | replay within valid window | nonce/event ID store |
| Same idempotency key different payload | Tampering | reuse key for different action | request fingerprint |
| Duplicate webhook processing | Tampering/DoS | provider retries cause double mutation | delivery ID dedupe |
| Secret hardcoded | Info Disclosure | repo/image leak | secret manager/scanning |
| Partner tenant spoofing | EoP | body says tenant B | trusted mapping table |

---

# 12. Threat Catalog - Cache/Revocation

| Threat | STRIDE | Attack/Failure | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stale allow | EoP | revoked permission still cached | versioned cache |
| Lost invalidation event | EoP | cache not evicted | TTL + version lookup |
| JWT permission stale | EoP | token carries old permission | short TTL, permission version |
| PDP timeout fail-open | EoP | dependency down allows request | fail-closed |
| Negative cache too long | Availability | new grant not effective | short TTL, grant invalidation |
| Cache key missing tenant | Info Disclosure | tenant A sees tenant B data | include tenant |
| Cache key missing resource/action | EoP | allow reused wrongly | include action/resource/version |

---

# 13. Abuse Cases

Abuse case lebih konkret daripada kategori STRIDE.

## Abuse Case 1 - BOLA Read

```text id="hawyfd"
As attacker Alice,
I change /cases/CASE-ALICE to /cases/CASE-BOB,
so I can read Bob's case.
```

Control:

```text id="2qyrbi"
- findByIdForTenant
- object-level canRead
- tenant-aware mapper
- 404 concealment for cross-tenant
- BOLA regression test
```

## Abuse Case 2 - Unauthorized Approval

```text id="g10c39"
As reviewer Alice,
I call POST /cases/CASE-BOB/approve,
so I can approve a case without supervisor permission.
```

Control:

```text id="6tqewl"
- @RequiresPermission("case.approve")
- object policy
- maker-checker rule
- SQL status/version guard
- audit deny
```

## Abuse Case 3 - Confused Deputy

```text id="th0sua"
As Alice without approve permission,
I ask orchestrator to approve a case,
so orchestrator uses its internal privilege to bypass my limitation.
```

Control:

```text id="aiqlel"
- delegated principal
- check calling service
- check original user
- downstream audience token
- actor chain audit
```

## Abuse Case 4 - Replay Partner Order

```text id="f3efg4"
As attacker with captured signed request,
I replay POST /partner/orders,
so order is created twice.
```

Control:

```text id="lgxyyx"
- timestamp
- nonce/event ID
- idempotency key
- request fingerprint
- DB unique constraint
```

---

# 14. Attack Tree - Cross-Tenant Data Leak

```mermaid id="qkngvz"
flowchart TD
    A[Goal: Read tenant B data as tenant A] --> B[Manipulate object ID]
    A --> C[Manipulate tenantId in request]
    A --> D[Exploit list/search endpoint]
    A --> E[Exploit cache key]
    A --> F[Exploit admin endpoint]
    A --> G[Exploit service endpoint]

    B --> B1[GET /cases/{tenantBCaseId}]
    B --> B2[GraphQL/global ID/base64 ID]
    B --> B3[File/download ID]

    C --> C1[tenantId in body]
    C --> C2[X-Tenant-ID header]
    C --> C3[query param tenantId]

    D --> D1[missing tenant predicate]
    D --> D2[count leakage]
    D --> D3[search filter overrides security filter]

    E --> E1[cache key case:{id}]
    E --> E2[visible list cache missing actor]

    F --> F1[admin role too broad]
    F --> F2[tenant admin not scoped]

    G --> G1[user token calls internal]
    G --> G2[service allowed all tenants]
```

Mitigation map:

```text id="l6kg5z"
tenant from principal/mapping, not body
findByIdForTenant
visibility criteria
cache key includes tenant+actor+version
admin tenant scope
internal service allowedTenants
audit cross-tenant deny
```

---

# 15. Attack Tree - Token Substitution

```mermaid id="j0fkrv"
flowchart TD
    A[Goal: Use invalid token to access API] --> B[Token for another audience]
    A --> C[Token from untrusted issuer]
    A --> D[ID token used as access token]
    A --> E[Expired token]
    A --> F[Token signed with attacker key]
    A --> G[alg confusion]
    A --> H[stolen bearer token replay]

    B --> B1[skip aud validation]
    C --> C1[loose issuer check]
    D --> D1[no token type check]
    E --> E1[skip exp/nbf]
    F --> F1[malicious JWKS/kid]
    G --> G1[accept none/HS-RS confusion]
    H --> H1[long TTL/no introspection]
```

Controls:

```text id="2jwvga"
issuer exact match
audience exact match
token type separation
exp/nbf/iat validation
JWKS trusted URI only
alg allowlist
short-lived token
mTLS/cert-bound token for high risk
```

---

# 16. Attack Tree - Privilege Escalation

```mermaid id="bby63g"
flowchart TD
    A[Goal: Gain higher privilege] --> B[Call admin endpoint]
    A --> C[Exploit role mapping]
    A --> D[Exploit stale cache]
    A --> E[Exploit impersonation]
    A --> F[Exploit workflow task]
    A --> G[Exploit service endpoint]

    B --> B1[endpoint unclassified]
    B --> B2[role ADMIN only]
    C --> C1[group membership bug]
    C --> C2[permission direct grant]
    D --> D1[role revoked but cache alive]
    E --> E1[missing grant/expiry]
    F --> F1[complete task not assigned]
    G --> G1[internal endpoint accepts user token]
```

Controls:

```text id="8kyw39"
route classification test
permission catalog
versioned entitlement
impersonation grant
workflow task policy
actorType guard
fresh check for sensitive action
```

---

# 17. DFD Checklist untuk Setiap Endpoint

Untuk endpoint baru, isi ini:

```text id="86t85n"
Endpoint:
  Method/path:
  Classification:
  Caller types:
  Credential type:
  Required scope:
  Required permission:
  Resource ID source:
  Tenant source:
  Object-level policy:
  State/workflow policy:
  MyBatis query guard:
  Cache behavior:
  Audit event:
  Error mapping:
  Negative tests:
```

Contoh:

```text id="m6p7jb"
Endpoint:
  POST /cases/{caseId}/approve

Classification:
  DOMAIN_PROTECTED

Caller:
  USER, delegated SERVICE on behalf of USER

Credential:
  Bearer access token

Permission:
  case.approve

Tenant source:
  principal.tenantContext

Object policy:
  same tenant
  status SUBMITTED
  actor != createdBy
  approval level >= risk
  assigned approver/supervisor

SQL guard:
  WHERE id=? AND tenant_id=? AND status='SUBMITTED' AND created_by <> ? AND version=?

Audit:
  AUTHORIZATION_DECISION
  CASE_APPROVED or BUSINESS_CONFLICT

Tests:
  missing permission
  wrong tenant
  maker-checker
  stale status
  delegated original user lacks permission
```

---

# 18. Threat-to-Control Matrix

| Threat | Prevent | Detect | Recover |
|---|---|---|---|
| BOLA | object auth, tenant query | cross-tenant deny alert | revoke leaked access, patch endpoint |
| Token substitution | issuer/audience validation | invalid_audience spike | rotate config/key if misused |
| Key confusion | trusted JWKS + alg allowlist | unknown kid spike | key rotation |
| Stale permission | versioned cache | stale version deny metric | invalidate cache/revoke token |
| Confused deputy | original actor check | delegated deny audit | fix orchestrator policy |
| Replay | nonce/idempotency | replay_detected metric | rotate secret if capture suspected |
| Impersonation abuse | grant + expiry + approval | impersonation audit review | revoke grant/session |
| Internal endpoint abuse | actorType SERVICE + mTLS | user-token-on-internal alert | block route/revoke token |
| Missing audit | mandatory audit tests | audit gap dashboard | backfill evidence if possible |
| PDP outage | fail-closed | pdp_timeout alert | restore service/cache |

---

# 19. Threat Modeling Questions per Layer

## Gateway / Nginx / Ingress

```text id="uw1of9"
- Apakah /internal/* exposed public?
- Apakah /admin/* punya route/origin/network khusus?
- Apakah spoofable identity headers di-strip?
- Apakah request size/rate limit ada?
- Apakah TLS/HSTS benar?
```

## Authentication Filter

```text id="tllp5p"
- Apakah token divalidasi signature/issuer/audience/expiry?
- Apakah ID token ditolak sebagai access token?
- Apakah JWKS URI trusted?
- Apakah alg allowlist?
- Apakah raw token tidak dilog?
```

## Principal/SecurityContext

```text id="e6li9v"
- Apakah actorType eksplisit?
- Apakah tenant source jelas?
- Apakah service vs user vs partner dibedakan?
- Apakah delegation/impersonation actor chain tidak hilang?
```

## Authorization Filter

```text id="v6aj87"
- Apakah endpoint classified?
- Apakah missing annotation fail closed?
- Apakah permission/scope dicek?
- Apakah internal endpoint menolak user token?
```

## Application Service

```text id="u0qrrt"
- Apakah object-level policy ada?
- Apakah state/workflow guard ada?
- Apakah tenant dari principal, bukan request body?
- Apakah sensitive action fresh entitlement?
```

## MyBatis/PostgreSQL

```text id="tjzwq3"
- Apakah SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE tenant-aware?
- Apakah list/count/search pakai visibility criteria sama?
- Apakah mutation punya status/version guard?
- Apakah bulk authorize per object?
```

---

# 20. Threat Modeling for Data Flows

NIST SP 800-154 menjelaskan data-centric threat modeling sebagai bentuk risk assessment yang memodelkan sisi attack dan defense untuk logical entity tertentu, termasuk data, application, host, system, atau environment; pendekatan ini berguna ketika fokus utama adalah melindungi jenis data tertentu. ([NIST Computer Security Resource Center](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/154/ipd?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Untuk authz, pilih data sensitif:

```text id="7ct7mc"
case
order
customer profile
workflow task
permission grant
audit event
token metadata
```

Lalu tanya:

```text id="eqhn3b"
Siapa boleh create/read/update/delete?
Dalam tenant mana?
Lewat endpoint apa?
Object ID berasal dari mana?
Query mana yang mengambil data?
Cache mana yang menyimpan data?
Audit mana yang mencatat akses?
```

---

# 21. Example - Threat Model untuk `GET /cases/{caseId}`

| Area | Threat | Control |
|---|---|---|
| Input | `caseId` diganti attacker | object-level auth |
| Tenant | case tenant beda | `findByIdForTenant`, 404 concealment |
| Token | token audience salah | JWT validator |
| Permission | user tidak punya read | `@RequiresPermission("case.read")` |
| Visibility | assigned/own/all salah | `CaseAccessPolicy.canRead` |
| Cache | response case cached by ID only | key includes tenant+actor |
| Error | 403 bocorkan existence | cross-tenant 404 |
| Audit | no evidence | authz decision audit |
| Test | only happy path | BOLA test |

---

# 22. Example - Threat Model untuk `POST /cases/{caseId}/approve`

| Area | Threat | Control |
|---|---|---|
| Function-level | reviewer call approve | `case.approve` permission |
| Object-level | approve case tenant lain | tenant guard |
| Maker-checker | creator approve own case | policy + SQL guard |
| State | approve closed/draft case | status guard |
| Race | state changes after check | optimistic lock/version |
| Delegation | service approves for unauthorized user | original actor check |
| Cache | revoked approve still allow | fresh entitlement |
| Audit | cannot prove approval | actor chain + old/new state |
| Error | state conflict shown as authz | 409 conflict |

---

# 23. Example - Threat Model untuk Internal Endpoint

Endpoint:

```text id="58kmrd"
POST /internal/cases/{caseId}/reindex
```

Threats:

```text id="3cz9u7"
- user token calls internal endpoint
- internal route exposed externally
- service token wrong audience
- service not allowed tenant
- replay reindex command
- service secret leaked
```

Controls:

```text id="xdq565"
- gateway blocks /internal/*
- @InternalEndpoint
- ActorType.SERVICE required
- mTLS/workload identity
- audience case-api
- internal.case.reindex permission
- allowedTenants check
- idempotency key
- service authorization audit
```

---

# 24. Example - Threat Model untuk Partner Webhook

Threats:

```text id="s596uj"
- fake webhook
- body tampering
- replay old webhook
- duplicate provider retry double-process
- partner tenant spoofing
- webhook secret leaked
```

Controls:

```text id="h7htb2"
- HMAC over raw body
- timestamp tolerance
- delivery ID dedupe
- idempotent business processing
- partner tenant mapping
- secret rotation
- payload size limit
- audit accepted/rejected event
```

---

# 25. Threat Modeling Permission Changes

Permission/role/ACL admin APIs adalah high-risk.

Threats:

```text id="nf3g7x"
- admin grants themselves privilege
- tenant admin grants cross-tenant role
- stale cache preserves revoked permission
- no audit of grant/revoke
- direct grant bypasses role governance
- expired ACL still active
```

Controls:

```text id="yk4v4s"
- admin permission specific
- step-up/MFA for high-risk change
- tenant-scoped admin policy
- grant reason/approval
- expires_at/revoked_at
- entitlement version increment
- audit old/new value
- alert outside business hours
```

---

# 26. Threat Modeling Cache

Ask:

```text id="n5qw9j"
- What if cached allow is stale?
- What if invalidation event is lost?
- What if version provider is down?
- What if cache key misses tenant?
- What if allow decision is reused for another action?
```

Controls:

```text id="8j6qo4"
- cache key includes tenant/subject/action/resource/version
- sensitive action fresh check
- TTL fallback
- versioned entitlement
- fail-closed on version lookup failure for sensitive action
- stale allow prevented metric
```

---

# 27. Threat Modeling Audit

Repudiation threats:

```text id="dn2zkl"
- user denies action
- admin denies role change
- support denies impersonation
- service action attributed to wrong user
- audit sink down during sensitive action
```

Controls:

```text id="brgkuq"
- correlation ID
- actor chain: real/effective/original/calling service
- policy name/version
- decision reason
- old/new state
- outbox/durable audit for sensitive action
- audit sink failure policy
```

---

# 28. Threat Modeling Secrets

Threats:

```text id="h6tghw"
- private key in app service
- API key committed to repo
- DB password in Docker image
- JWKS malicious URL
- old HMAC secret still active
- secret printed in MyBatis/JDBC logs
```

Controls:

```text id="js38b7"
- secret manager/KMS/Vault
- GitHub secret scanning/push protection
- no secret in image/config repo
- trusted JWKS URI
- rotation overlap + revoke
- log redaction
- least privilege secret access
```

---

# 29. Risk Scoring Simple Model

Gunakan skor sederhana agar threat model actionable.

| Factor | 1 | 3 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Impact | minor | tenant data leak | multi-tenant/admin compromise |
| Likelihood | hard/internal | plausible | easy/public |
| Detectability | obvious | some logs | silent |
| Control maturity | strong/tested | partial | missing |

Priority:

```text id="m1ilxs"
risk = impact + likelihood + detectability + control_gap
```

Interpretation:

```text id="z4r4mg"
16-20:
  fix before release

11-15:
  fix soon, add compensating control

6-10:
  track and test

<=5:
  accept with rationale
```

---

# 30. Threat Register Template

```text id="fe91hk"
Threat ID:
Title:
Endpoint/component:
STRIDE category:
Abuse case:
Preconditions:
Attack steps:
Impact:
Likelihood:
Existing controls:
Missing controls:
Decision:
Owner:
Due date:
Tests required:
Audit/metrics required:
Status:
```

Example:

```text id="cnsj5b"
Threat ID:
  AUTHZ-001

Title:
  BOLA on GET /cases/{caseId}

STRIDE:
  Elevation of Privilege, Information Disclosure

Attack:
  User changes caseId to another user's case.

Impact:
  Tenant/customer data leak.

Existing controls:
  @RequiresPermission("case.read")

Missing controls:
  Object-level canRead test; mapper tenant predicate test.

Decision:
  Must fix before release.

Tests:
  cross-tenant 404
  same-tenant unassigned 403
  assigned allow
```

---

# 31. Threat Modeling Output yang Bagus

Output minimum:

```text id="4wln2i"
1. Data flow diagram
2. Trust boundaries
3. Actor list
4. Asset list
5. Threat register
6. Control mapping
7. Test plan
8. Open risks
9. Owners/due dates
10. Go/no-go recommendation
```

Jangan berhenti di diagram saja. Threat model harus menghasilkan action engineering.

---

# 32. Secure Design Review Checklist

```text id="txmar2"
[ ] Endpoint classification exists.
[ ] Credential type clear.
[ ] Actor type clear.
[ ] Token issuer/audience/expiry validated.
[ ] Tenant source explicit.
[ ] Object ID treated as hostile.
[ ] Object-level authorization exists.
[ ] List/search/count visibility consistent.
[ ] Mutation has tenant/status/version guard.
[ ] Bulk operation authorizes per object.
[ ] Delegation checks service and original user.
[ ] Impersonation requires grant and audit.
[ ] Cache freshness strategy documented.
[ ] Revocation path documented.
[ ] Audit event defined.
[ ] Error mapping safe.
[ ] Negative tests defined.
```

---

# 33. Threat-Driven Test Plan

| Threat | Test |
|---|---|
| BOLA | user A requests user B object |
| Cross-tenant leak | tenant A requests tenant B ID |
| Missing permission | token valid but no permission |
| Wrong audience | token for other API |
| ID token misuse | ID token sent as access token |
| Stale cache | revoke permission then sensitive action |
| Confused deputy | service call with unauthorized original user |
| Impersonation abuse | no grant/expired grant |
| Replay | same nonce twice |
| Idempotency mismatch | same key different payload |
| Internal endpoint exposed | user token calls `/internal/*` |
| Error disclosure | invalid token no stack/token detail |

---

# 34. Threat Modeling Cadence

| Moment | Activity |
|---|---|
| New endpoint | mini threat model |
| New auth mechanism | full auth flow threat model |
| New permission/role design | entitlement threat review |
| New partner integration | API key/HMAC/replay model |
| New internal endpoint | service identity review |
| New cache/PDP | consistency/fail-closed review |
| Before release | production readiness threat review |
| After incident | update threat model with learned abuse case |

---

# 35. Engineering Artifacts

Threat model should link to:

```text id="g3q7ah"
- OpenAPI spec
- sequence diagram
- permission catalog
- endpoint classification list
- MyBatis mapper
- policy classes
- audit schema
- test classes
- runbook
- dashboard/alerts
```

Good sign:

```text id="emjtxv"
A developer can go from threat -> control -> code -> test -> alert.
```

---

# 36. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Risiko | Perbaikan |
|---|---|---|
| Threat model dibuat setelah release | too late | model during design |
| Hanya bahas network | business authz miss | include object/tenant/state |
| STRIDE checklist tanpa action | no engineering value | produce test/control items |
| “Gateway handles auth” | object auth missing | service-layer policy |
| Tidak model cache/revocation | stale allow | versioned cache |
| Tidak model audit | repudiation gap | actor chain audit |
| Tidak model internal endpoint | lateral movement | service identity/mTLS |
| Tidak model partner/webhook | replay/tampering | HMAC/nonce/idempotency |
| Tidak test abuse cases | regressions | security regression suite |
| Tidak update threat model | stale assumptions | cadence + ownership |

---

# 37. Minimal Production Invariants

```text id="fb4782"
1. Every sensitive endpoint has an abuse case.
2. Every object ID from client is modeled as attacker-controlled.
3. Every tenant boundary is explicit.
4. Every service-to-service call has caller identity and audience validation.
5. Every delegated call checks service and original actor.
6. Every impersonation path has grant, expiry, reason, and audit.
7. Every cache-based authorization path has revocation strategy.
8. Every security decision has audit evidence.
9. Every high-risk threat maps to at least one test.
10. Threat model findings must become backlog/PR checklist items.
```

---

# 38. Roadmap Singkat

| Urutan | Fokus | Target |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | DFD & trust boundary | Paham alur identity/token/permission/data |
| 2 | STRIDE | Threat taxonomy konsisten |
| 3 | Asset & actor inventory | Tahu yang dilindungi dan siapa caller |
| 4 | Abuse cases | Threat konkret seperti attacker story |
| 5 | Attack trees | Threat kompleks dipecah jalurnya |
| 6 | Control mapping | Prevent/detect/recover jelas |
| 7 | Threat register | Ada owner, priority, due date |
| 8 | Threat-driven tests | Abuse case jadi regression test |
| 9 | Release gate | High risk tidak lolos tanpa control |
| 10 | Continuous update | Threat model ikut berubah bersama sistem |

---

Lanjutkan ke: **Cheatsheet Secure Design Review Checklist for Authentication/Authorization in Jersey/JAX-RS - PR checklist, architecture review, endpoint review, mapper review, policy review, and release gate**.
