## Response:
7/13/2026, 5:25:45 AM

# Cheatsheet - Secure Design Review Checklist untuk Authentication/Authorization di Jersey/JAX-RS

## 1. Tujuan

Secure design review adalah gate sebelum implementation/PR/release untuk memastikan desain authN/authZ tidak “kelihatan aman” tetapi bolong di object-level, tenant boundary, delegation, cache, audit, atau query.

Fokus review:

```text id="z3zv98"
- Apakah endpoint sudah diklasifikasi?
- Siapa caller yang valid?
- Credential apa yang diterima?
- Permission/scope apa yang dibutuhkan?
- Tenant berasal dari mana?
- Object-level authorization di mana?
- Query MyBatis/PostgreSQL sudah tenant-aware?
- Cache/revocation aman?
- Error response tidak bocor?
- Audit cukup untuk forensic?
- Negative tests sudah ada?
```

OWASP ASVS menyediakan basis untuk menguji technical security controls dan juga requirement secure development; OWASP Code Review Guide ditujukan untuk reviewer yang perlu menemukan vulnerability dalam code review; dan OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet menekankan least privilege, deny by default, serta validasi permission pada setiap request. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/www-project-application-security-verification-standard/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Review Flow

```mermaid id="9c89vo"
flowchart TD
    A[New Feature / Endpoint / PR] --> B[Endpoint Classification]
    B --> C[Authentication Review]
    C --> D[Principal & Tenant Review]
    D --> E[Authorization Review]
    E --> F[Object-Level Review]
    F --> G[MyBatis/PostgreSQL Review]
    G --> H[Cache/Revocation Review]
    H --> I[Audit/Error Review]
    I --> J[Test Review]
    J --> K{Risk Accepted?}

    K -->|No| L[Block PR / Redesign]
    K -->|Yes| M[Approve with Checklist Evidence]
```

---

# 3. Review Severity

| Severity | Meaning | PR Action |
|---|---|---|
| **Blocker** | Bisa menyebabkan auth bypass, tenant leak, privilege escalation, secret leak | PR tidak boleh merge |
| **High** | Security control penting tidak lengkap/test kurang | Fix sebelum release |
| **Medium** | Defense-in-depth kurang, observability/test kurang | Fix soon / tracked |
| **Low** | Naming/docs/minor consistency | Bisa follow-up |
| **Accepted Risk** | Ada alasan bisnis/teknis dan mitigasi kompensasi | Harus terdokumentasi |

Rule:

```text id="5h7drf"
Jika reviewer tidak bisa menjawab "siapa boleh melakukan apa terhadap object apa dalam tenant apa",
desain belum siap.
```

---

# 4. Design Review Template

Gunakan template ini untuk endpoint/feature baru.

```text id="rsoglb"
Feature:
Endpoint(s):
Classification:
Caller type(s):
Credential type:
AuthN mechanism:
Required OAuth scope:
Required internal permission:
Tenant source:
Resource ID source:
Object-level policy:
Workflow/state policy:
Delegation/impersonation behavior:
Service-to-service behavior:
Partner/webhook behavior:
MyBatis/PostgreSQL guard:
Cache/revocation behavior:
Audit event(s):
Error semantics:
Security headers/CORS/CSRF impact:
Secrets/key impact:
Negative tests:
Open risks:
Reviewer decision:
```

---

# 5. Endpoint Classification Checklist

Setiap endpoint harus masuk salah satu kategori.

| Classification | Contoh | Required Control |
|---|---|---|
| `PUBLIC` | `/health/live`, public docs | intentionally public, sanitized |
| `AUTHENTICATED` | `/me` | valid token/session |
| `DOMAIN_PROTECTED` | `/cases/{id}` | permission + object policy |
| `ADMIN` | `/admin/users/{id}/disable` | admin permission + step-up + audit |
| `INTERNAL_SERVICE` | `/internal/reindex` | service actor + mTLS/internal scope |
| `PARTNER` | `/partner/orders` | partner auth + tenant mapping |
| `WEBHOOK` | `/webhooks/provider` | HMAC/signature + dedupe |
| `OPERATIONS` | `/metrics`, `/ready` | network/auth boundary |
| `BREAK_GLASS` | emergency access | approval + expiry + alert |

Checklist:

```text id="fr6jmx"
[ ] Endpoint classification explicit.
[ ] Missing classification fails test/build.
[ ] Public endpoint has documented reason.
[ ] Internal/admin/ops endpoint not exposed through public route.
[ ] Classification appears in OpenAPI/doc or route registry.
[ ] Security annotation matches classification.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="5u0gqa"
- New endpoint without classification.
- /internal/* reachable externally without service auth.
- /admin/* protected only by generic authenticated user.
```

---

# 6. Authentication Review Checklist

For Bearer/JWT/OIDC:

```text id="g2fpl1"
[ ] Token signature is validated.
[ ] Issuer is exact-match allowlisted.
[ ] Audience is exact-match target API.
[ ] Expiry (`exp`) is required and checked.
[ ] `nbf` is checked if present.
[ ] Algorithm allowlist exists.
[ ] `alg=none` is rejected.
[ ] JWKS URI is trusted/configured, not token-controlled.
[ ] Unknown `kid` refreshes JWKS once, then denies.
[ ] ID token is not accepted as API access token.
[ ] Raw token is never logged.
```

For API key/HMAC:

```text id="ox19k6"
[ ] API key is not accepted in query string.
[ ] Secret/API key is not stored plaintext.
[ ] HMAC covers canonical request + raw body digest.
[ ] Timestamp tolerance exists.
[ ] Nonce/event ID replay protection exists.
[ ] Constant-time signature comparison is used.
[ ] Secret rotation supports old/new overlap.
```

For service-to-service:

```text id="u431k4"
[ ] Actor type SERVICE is required for internal endpoint.
[ ] Token audience is target API.
[ ] Client/service identity is registered.
[ ] Internal scope/permission is granular.
[ ] mTLS/workload identity is required for high-privilege service calls.
```

OWASP API Security 2023 lists Broken Authentication as API2, and OWASP API1 Broken Object Level Authorization notes that attackers commonly manipulate object IDs in path, query, headers, or payload. These two risks often appear together when token validation is correct but object authorization is missing. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/www-project-api-security/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 7. Principal & SecurityContext Review

Checklist:

```text id="w0fz0s"
[ ] Resource method does not parse JWT manually.
[ ] `SecurityContext` is set by authentication filter.
[ ] Principal model has explicit actor type.
[ ] USER, SERVICE, PARTNER, ADMIN, SYSTEM are distinguishable.
[ ] Tenant context is explicit.
[ ] TokenContext stores metadata only, not raw token.
[ ] TransportContext does not trust spoofable headers from internet.
[ ] Delegation preserves calling service and original actor.
[ ] Impersonation preserves real actor and effective actor.
[ ] Principal is immutable.
[ ] Tests cover principal mapper for each actor type.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="biztmy"
- Resource method reads Authorization header and parses JWT.
- Service token and user token map to same ambiguous principal.
- Impersonation overwrites real actor.
```

---

# 8. Authorization Review Checklist

Endpoint-level:

```text id="d7hvf3"
[ ] Endpoint has required scope/permission annotation.
[ ] Missing annotation is not treated as public.
[ ] Authorization filter runs after authentication.
[ ] Permission names come from permission catalog.
[ ] Role is not used as final authorization unless explicitly justified.
[ ] Deny-by-default behavior exists.
```

Application service:

```text id="31lzpm"
[ ] Service layer receives `SecurityActor`, not raw token.
[ ] Domain action has policy method.
[ ] Policy returns decision object with reason/policy/version.
[ ] Sensitive action requires fresh/version-validated entitlement.
[ ] Decision is audited before throwing/performing mutation.
```

OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet recommends enforcing least privilege, denying by default, and validating permissions on every request; code review must verify those rules exist in the actual endpoint/service path, not only in documentation. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 9. Object-Level Authorization Review

Checklist:

```text id="olmol0"
[ ] Every object ID from path/query/header/body is treated as hostile.
[ ] Endpoint does not rely only on global permission.
[ ] Object is loaded with tenant boundary where possible.
[ ] Object-level policy checks ownership/assignment/ACL/relationship/state.
[ ] Cross-tenant existence is concealed if required.
[ ] List/search/count use same visibility criteria.
[ ] Bulk operation authorizes per object or uses safe scoped SQL.
[ ] File/download/export endpoints have object authorization.
[ ] Tests include same-tenant denied and cross-tenant denied.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="hd1b6e"
- `findById(caseId)` without tenant/object policy.
- `case.read` allows reading all cases unintentionally.
- Count/search endpoint returns data outside visibility filter.
```

OWASP describes BOLA as attackers manipulating an object identifier in request target, headers, or payload; therefore every function that accesses a data source using a user-supplied ID must consider object-level authorization. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 10. Tenant Isolation Review

Checklist:

```text id="5eqsdt"
[ ] Tenant source is explicit: token claim, service registry, partner mapping, resource lookup, or job scope.
[ ] Tenant ID from request body/query/header is not trusted directly.
[ ] MyBatis SELECT includes tenant predicate unless intentionally global.
[ ] UPDATE/DELETE includes tenant predicate.
[ ] Admin endpoint is tenant-scoped unless platform-admin explicitly allowed.
[ ] Partner external tenant ref is mapped to internal tenant ID.
[ ] Service allowedTenants/tenant scope is checked.
[ ] Cache keys include tenant.
[ ] Audit includes tenant ID.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="j7kma8"
- `WHERE id = #{id}` for tenant-scoped table.
- Partner request body chooses internal tenant ID.
- Cache key uses only `case:{caseId}`.
```

---

# 11. MyBatis/PostgreSQL Mapper Review

SELECT checklist:

```text id="w3fzij"
[ ] Tenant-scoped table has `tenant_id = #{tenantId}`.
[ ] List/search query includes mandatory security filters.
[ ] User filters are additive, not replacing security filters.
[ ] Count query uses same visibility criteria as list query.
[ ] Mapper does not generate `WHERE ()` or missing WHERE due empty lists.
[ ] Dynamic SQL branch has deny-by-default fallback.
```

UPDATE/DELETE checklist:

```text id="k9u1kb"
[ ] `UPDATE` and `DELETE` include tenant predicate.
[ ] State-changing update includes expected status/state.
[ ] Optimistic lock/version guard exists where race matters.
[ ] Maker-checker guard is in policy and/or SQL.
[ ] Bulk update is constrained by tenant and authorized IDs.
[ ] Update count 0 maps to 404/409/403 correctly.
```

MyBatis dynamic SQL provides elements such as `if`, `choose`, `where`, `set`, and `foreach`; these are useful, but reviewer must ensure branches cannot accidentally remove mandatory security predicates. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example review smell:

```xml id="k19g5l"
<select id="findCases">
  SELECT * FROM cases
  <where>
    <if test="tenantId != null">
      tenant_id = #{tenantId}
    </if>
  </where>
</select>
```

Problem:

```text id="qyytkl"
If tenantId is null, query may return all tenants.
```

Better:

```xml id="uqukmh"
<select id="findCases">
  SELECT *
  FROM cases
  WHERE tenant_id = #{tenantId}
</select>
```

And validate `tenantId` is never null before mapper call.

---

# 12. Permission Catalog Review

Checklist:

```text id="qvtjvj"
[ ] New permission exists in catalog/migration.
[ ] Permission name is business capability, not UI control.
[ ] Risk level is assigned.
[ ] Role-permission mapping is intentional.
[ ] Role does not bundle unrelated high-risk permissions.
[ ] Direct user grant has reason, expiry, audit.
[ ] Service permission uses internal/system namespace.
[ ] Partner entitlement is separate from user role.
[ ] Permission removal/revoke increments entitlement version.
```

Bad:

```text id="0sg8um"
permission = showApproveButton
role = SUPER_ADMIN_DO_EVERYTHING
service uses case.approve user permission
```

Better:

```text id="8qbk4z"
case.approve
admin.role.assign
internal.case.reindex
partner.order.create
```

---

# 13. Delegation / Impersonation Review

Delegation checklist:

```text id="vfyc4b"
[ ] Calling service is identified.
[ ] Original actor is identified.
[ ] Target API validates audience.
[ ] Calling service is authorized for delegated action.
[ ] Original actor is authorized for object/action.
[ ] Delegated token is scoped down.
[ ] Delegation chain depth is limited.
[ ] Audit stores calling service + original actor + effective actor.
```

Impersonation checklist:

```text id="kquv68"
[ ] Explicit active impersonation grant exists.
[ ] Grant has reason/ticket/approver/expiry.
[ ] Allowed actions are limited.
[ ] Real actor is never overwritten.
[ ] Effective actor is clear.
[ ] Impersonation action is audited and reviewable.
[ ] High-risk mutation may be disallowed under impersonation.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="8dbt9b"
- Service approves case using only service permission, ignoring original user.
- Support admin “becomes” user without trace of support admin.
```

---

# 14. Cache / Revocation Review

Checklist:

```text id="yhi67v"
[ ] Authorization cache key includes tenant and subject.
[ ] Object/ACL cache key includes resource and action.
[ ] Effective permission cache is versioned.
[ ] Revocation increments entitlement/resource version.
[ ] Invalidation event/outbox exists for role/permission/ACL changes.
[ ] TTL matches risk level.
[ ] Sensitive write does not rely on stale cached allow.
[ ] PDP/entitlement failure fails closed for sensitive action.
[ ] Token permission version is checked if permissions are token-embedded.
[ ] Tests cover revoke -> immediate/fresh deny path.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="efg72o"
- Admin permission revoke does not invalidate cache.
- `case.approve` uses cached allow for minutes after revoke.
- Cache key missing tenant.
```

---

# 15. Error Handling Review

Checklist:

```text id="49b9n7"
[ ] AuthN failure maps to 401.
[ ] AuthZ failure maps to 403 unless concealment policy uses 404.
[ ] Cross-tenant object existence does not leak unintentionally.
[ ] 401 Bearer response includes `WWW-Authenticate`.
[ ] Error body follows stable format.
[ ] Error response includes correlation ID.
[ ] Response does not expose stack trace, SQL, token, permission internals.
[ ] Audit contains internal reason.
[ ] Fallback exception mapper is generic and safe.
```

OWASP ASVS error/logging objective emphasizes useful information for users, administrators, and incident response teams, not excessive noisy logs; security review should check that user-facing error details and internal audit details are separated. ([GitHub](https://github.com/OWASP/ASVS/blob/master/4.0/en/0x15-V7-Error-Logging.md?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 16. Audit Review

Checklist:

```text id="r4q35g"
[ ] Authentication success/failure audited where appropriate.
[ ] Authorization allow/deny audited for sensitive action.
[ ] Cross-tenant deny has internal audit.
[ ] Permission/role/ACL changes audited with old/new values.
[ ] Impersonation/delegation actor chain audited.
[ ] Service/partner calls include clientId/serviceName/partnerId.
[ ] Audit event includes correlationId.
[ ] Audit event includes policy name/version.
[ ] Raw token/secret/request body not logged.
[ ] Audit sink failure behavior is defined.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="x0zfhq"
- Admin role assignment has no audit.
- Break-glass access does not alert.
- Impersonation audit only shows effective user.
```

---

# 17. Secrets / Key Review

Checklist:

```text id="kk65pv"
[ ] No secret in code, config repo, test resource, Dockerfile, Helm plaintext.
[ ] Secret reference is used instead of secret value.
[ ] API keys stored hashed/protected, not plaintext.
[ ] JWT private signing key is not in resource server.
[ ] JWKS URI is configured/trusted.
[ ] Algorithm allowlist exists.
[ ] Rotation plan supports overlap if clients need migration.
[ ] Logs/errors/metrics redact secret/token.
[ ] Kubernetes/Vault/KMS access is least privilege.
[ ] CI secret scanning/push protection is enabled.
```

GitHub push protection is designed to prevent supported secrets from being pushed to repositories, and OWASP Secrets Management gives guidance around secure lifecycle and rotation. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-coding-practices-quick-reference-guide/stable-en/02-checklist/05-checklist?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 18. Browser/Gateway Boundary Review

Checklist:

```text id="d6wv8d"
[ ] CORS origin allowlist is explicit.
[ ] Credentialed CORS does not use wildcard.
[ ] `Vary: Origin` is set for dynamic CORS.
[ ] Cookie-authenticated unsafe methods require CSRF token.
[ ] Auth cookies use Secure, HttpOnly, appropriate SameSite.
[ ] Admin/internal/ops origins are separate from public app.
[ ] Gateway strips spoofable identity headers.
[ ] Gateway blocks public access to internal/ops endpoints.
[ ] Service-level authorization still exists behind gateway.
[ ] Security headers are present on normal and error responses.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="lrqqha"
- `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` reflects any Origin for authenticated API.
- CSRF skipped for cookie-authenticated POST.
- Trusting `X-Forwarded-User` from public internet.
```

---

# 19. Webhook / Partner / Replay Review

Checklist:

```text id="3mp069"
[ ] Webhook signature is verified before parsing business payload.
[ ] Signature uses raw body bytes.
[ ] Timestamp tolerance exists.
[ ] Nonce/delivery ID replay protection exists.
[ ] Duplicate webhook delivery is idempotent.
[ ] Idempotency key required for unsafe retryable operation.
[ ] Same idempotency key + different payload is rejected.
[ ] Partner tenant resolved from trusted mapping.
[ ] Payload size limit exists.
[ ] Invalid signature/replay/idempotency conflict audited.
```

---

# 20. Workflow/Camunda Review

Checklist:

```text id="a645bi"
[ ] Completing task requires permission.
[ ] User must be assignee/candidate/group or authorized supervisor.
[ ] Business object visibility is checked.
[ ] Process instance/business key tenant matches actor tenant.
[ ] State transition is valid.
[ ] Maker-checker rule applies where needed.
[ ] Escalation/reassignment has separate permission.
[ ] System task is attributed to system/process, not fake user.
[ ] Camunda task assignment is not the only authorization control.
[ ] Audit links processInstanceId, taskId, businessKey, actor.
```

Blocker examples:

```text id="wriut0"
- Any authenticated user can complete task by taskId.
- API trusts taskId without checking business object tenant.
```

---

# 21. OpenAPI / Contract Review

Checklist:

```text id="do80xa"
[ ] Each operation declares security scheme.
[ ] Each operation has classification metadata.
[ ] Required scopes are documented if public API contract.
[ ] Error responses include 401/403/404/409/422 where relevant.
[ ] Idempotency header documented for unsafe retryable operation.
[ ] Webhook signature headers documented.
[ ] Partner tenant external ref documented.
[ ] Admin/internal endpoints not published in public docs.
```

Example extension:

```yaml id="0xgbwt"
paths:
  /cases/{caseId}/approve:
    post:
      x-endpoint-classification: DOMAIN_PROTECTED
      x-required-permissions:
        - case.approve
      x-object-authorization:
        resourceType: case
        resourceIdParam: caseId
      security:
        - bearerAuth: []
      responses:
        "204":
          description: Approved
        "401":
          description: Unauthorized
        "403":
          description: Access denied
        "409":
          description: State conflict
```

---

# 22. PR Review Checklist - Compact Version

Gunakan ini untuk PR sehari-hari.

```text id="po3rg1"
Endpoint & route
[ ] New/changed endpoint classified.
[ ] Public/internal/admin/partner boundary correct.

Authentication
[ ] Credential validation correct.
[ ] Token/API key/signature not parsed ad hoc.

Principal
[ ] SecurityActor used, not raw JWT.
[ ] Actor type and tenant explicit.

Authorization
[ ] Permission/scope required.
[ ] Object-level policy applied.
[ ] Tenant/resource/state checks applied.

Data access
[ ] MyBatis SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE tenant-aware.
[ ] List/count/search use same visibility.

Cache/revocation
[ ] Cache key includes tenant/subject/resource/version where needed.
[ ] Sensitive action fresh/version-validated.

Audit/error
[ ] Decision audited.
[ ] Error response safe with correlationId.

Tests
[ ] Negative tests cover missing permission, wrong tenant, wrong object.
```

---

# 23. Architecture Review Checklist - Larger Change

For new service/module:

```text id="ypjosg"
[ ] Data flow diagram exists.
[ ] Trust boundaries identified.
[ ] Actor types listed.
[ ] Endpoint taxonomy listed.
[ ] Permission catalog changes listed.
[ ] Tenant model described.
[ ] AuthN mechanism described.
[ ] AuthZ policy architecture described.
[ ] Object-level authorization strategy described.
[ ] MyBatis/PostgreSQL enforcement described.
[ ] Cache/revocation model described.
[ ] Audit/event schema described.
[ ] Secret/key dependencies listed.
[ ] Threat model done for high-risk flows.
[ ] Test strategy includes abuse cases.
[ ] Operational alerts/runbook listed.
```

---

# 24. Release Gate Checklist

Before release/go-live:

```text id="e4wd1g"
[ ] No unclassified endpoints.
[ ] No known critical/high auth bypass findings.
[ ] All admin/internal/partner endpoints reviewed.
[ ] BOLA tests pass.
[ ] Tenant isolation tests pass.
[ ] Authentication negative tests pass.
[ ] Authorization negative tests pass.
[ ] Revocation/cache tests pass.
[ ] Audit events verified in staging.
[ ] Secret scan clean.
[ ] OpenAPI security contract reviewed.
[ ] Gateway route policy reviewed.
[ ] CORS/CSRF/header tests pass if browser-facing.
[ ] Rollback plan exists.
[ ] Monitoring alerts configured.
```

---

# 25. Reviewer Red Flags

Immediate pause/rework if you see:

```text id="bf7qtl"
- `if (role.equals("ADMIN"))` scattered in code.
- `SecurityContext` ignored and JWT parsed manually.
- `findById(id)` on tenant-scoped resource.
- `UPDATE table SET ... WHERE id = ?`.
- `tenantId` accepted from request body as trusted.
- `/internal` endpoint accepts user token.
- CORS reflects arbitrary Origin.
- API key accepted in query param.
- Raw token/API key in log.
- Permission cache with long TTL and no version.
- Impersonation without grant/audit.
- Webhook processed before signature verification.
```

---

# 26. Example Review - Bad Endpoint

```java id="1me2cb"
@POST
@Path("/cases/{caseId}/approve")
public Response approve(
        @HeaderParam("Authorization") String token,
        @PathParam("caseId") String caseId
) {
    Claims claims = Jwt.parse(token);
    if (!claims.getRoles().contains("ADMIN")) {
        throw new ForbiddenException();
    }

    caseMapper.approve(caseId);
    return Response.noContent().build();
}
```

Findings:

```text id="3jhw4j"
BLOCKER: manual JWT parsing in resource.
BLOCKER: no signature/issuer/audience shown.
BLOCKER: role ADMIN final policy.
BLOCKER: no tenant boundary.
BLOCKER: no object-level policy.
BLOCKER: update by ID only.
HIGH: no audit.
HIGH: no maker-checker/state guard.
HIGH: no tests indicated.
```

Better shape:

```java id="l3v7qt"
@POST
@Path("/cases/{caseId}/approve")
@RequiresPermission("case.approve")
public Response approve(
        @Context SecurityContext securityContext,
        @PathParam("caseId") String caseId
) {
    SecurityActor actor = SecurityPrincipals.requireActor(securityContext);

    caseApplicationService.approveCase(actor, caseId);

    return Response.noContent().build();
}
```

---

# 27. Example Review - Good Service Pattern

```java id="vy89aj"
public void approveCase(SecurityActor actor, String caseId) {
    CaseRecord caseRecord = caseRepository.findByIdForTenant(
            caseId,
            actor.tenantContext().tenantId()
    ).orElseThrow(() -> new NotFoundException("Case not found"));

    Set<String> freshPermissions =
            entitlementService.effectivePermissions(
                    actor,
                    AuthorizationFreshnessLevel.FRESH_REQUIRED
            );

    AuthorizationDecision decision =
            casePolicy.canApprove(actor, freshPermissions, caseRecord);

    auditLogger.logAuthorizationDecision(actor, "case.approve", caseRecord, decision);

    if (!decision.allowed()) {
        throw new AccessDeniedException(decision.reason());
    }

    int updated = caseRepository.approveIfStillEligible(
            caseId,
            caseRecord.tenantId(),
            actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
            caseRecord.version()
    );

    if (updated == 0) {
        throw new BusinessConflictException(
                "The resource state changed. Please refresh and try again.",
                "case_approve_update_count_zero"
        );
    }
}
```

Review result:

```text id="kbwt1m"
PASS:
  endpoint permission
  tenant-aware load
  fresh entitlement for sensitive action
  object/state policy
  audit before mutation
  SQL update guard expected
```

---

# 28. Mapper Review Example

Bad:

```xml id="emukfw"
<update id="approve">
    UPDATE cases
    SET status = 'APPROVED'
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
</update>
```

Finding:

```text id="95kxka"
BLOCKER:
  missing tenant_id
  missing expected status
  missing maker-checker guard
  missing optimistic version
```

Better:

```xml id="a6mhvn"
<update id="approveIfStillEligible">
    UPDATE cases
    SET status = 'APPROVED',
        approved_by = #{actorId},
        approved_at = now(),
        version = version + 1
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
      AND tenant_id = #{tenantId}
      AND status = 'SUBMITTED'
      AND created_by <> #{actorId}
      AND version = #{expectedVersion}
</update>
```

---

# 29. Test Review Checklist

Minimum negative tests:

```text id="lv6jrm"
Authentication:
[ ] missing token
[ ] malformed token
[ ] expired token
[ ] wrong issuer/audience

Endpoint authz:
[ ] valid token missing permission
[ ] user token to internal endpoint
[ ] service token to user-only endpoint

Object/tenant:
[ ] wrong tenant object
[ ] same tenant but not owner/assignee/ACL
[ ] list/count/search visibility

Mutation:
[ ] invalid state
[ ] maker-checker violation
[ ] version conflict
[ ] bulk partially denied

Delegation/impersonation:
[ ] original actor lacks permission
[ ] impersonation grant missing/expired

Cache/revocation:
[ ] revoke permission then sensitive action denied
[ ] stale token permission version denied

Audit/error:
[ ] deny audited
[ ] response does not leak token/SQL/stack
```

OWASP Code Review Guide frames secure review as identifying vulnerabilities throughout review; the checklist should therefore ask for negative tests that prove the vulnerability class is closed, not only happy-path unit tests. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/www-project-code-review-guide/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 30. Security Acceptance Criteria

For each user story/API story, add explicit security AC.

Example:

```text id="6vrjpp"
Story:
  As a supervisor, I can approve a submitted case.

Security acceptance criteria:
  - Only users with case.approve can call approval endpoint.
  - User cannot approve own created case.
  - User cannot approve case outside tenant.
  - Case must be SUBMITTED.
  - Revoked case.approve is denied for approval.
  - Approval decision is audited with actor, tenant, caseId, policy version.
  - Cross-tenant caseId returns 404 and logs internal deny.
```

---

# 31. Review Output Format

```text id="x0l0nk"
Decision:
  APPROVE / APPROVE WITH FOLLOW-UP / BLOCK

Summary:
  One-paragraph risk summary.

Findings:
  [BLOCKER] ...
  [HIGH] ...
  [MEDIUM] ...

Required changes:
  1. ...
  2. ...

Tests required:
  1. ...
  2. ...

Accepted risks:
  - ...
```

Example:

```text id="g3xm38"
Decision:
  BLOCK

Summary:
  Endpoint validates user permission but does not enforce object-level access or tenant-aware update.

Findings:
  [BLOCKER] MyBatis update uses WHERE id only.
  [BLOCKER] No object-level policy for case.approve.
  [HIGH] No audit event for denied approval.

Required changes:
  Add findByIdForTenant, CaseAccessPolicy.canApprove, approveIfStillEligible SQL guard, and negative tests.
```

---

# 32. Reviewer Role Split

| Reviewer | Focus |
|---|---|
| Feature owner | business rules, workflow state |
| Senior backend engineer | Jersey/service/MyBatis correctness |
| Security reviewer | threat/control coverage |
| DBA/data engineer | SQL/index/tenant enforcement |
| SRE/platform | gateway, secrets, observability |
| QA | negative/regression coverage |

For high-risk authz changes, do not rely on a single reviewer.

---

# 33. Common Review Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Risk | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Review only annotation | object auth missing | trace to service and mapper |
| Trust happy-path tests | bypass not tested | require negative tests |
| Treat gateway as final authz | service bypass/lateral movement | service-layer policy |
| Approve role check | privilege too broad | permission/object policy |
| Ignore query | tenant leak | mapper review |
| Ignore cache | stale allow | revocation tests |
| Ignore audit | no forensic evidence | audit AC |
| Ignore error response | information disclosure | secure error tests |
| “Will fix later” blocker | release risk | block or risk acceptance |

---

# 34. Design Review Scoring

Use a simple score to prioritize.

| Area | 0 | 1 | 2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Endpoint classified | no | partial | yes |
| AuthN validated | no | partial | complete |
| Permission defined | no | role only | permission catalog |
| Object auth | no | partial | complete |
| Tenant guard | no | app only | app + SQL |
| Cache/revoke | no | TTL only | version+event |
| Audit | no | partial | complete |
| Tests | happy only | some negative | full abuse tests |

Interpretation:

```text id="99q10n"
0-7:
  block

8-11:
  high risk, fix before release

12-14:
  acceptable with follow-up

15-16:
  strong
```

---

# 35. CI/Automation Ideas

Automatable checks:

```text id="phdw9j"
[ ] Reflection test: every JAX-RS method classified.
[ ] Static scan: no `@Path` method without security annotation.
[ ] Static scan: no manual `Authorization` header parsing in resource.
[ ] Static scan: MyBatis update/delete on tenant tables includes tenant_id.
[ ] Unit test: permission catalog references valid permissions.
[ ] OpenAPI check: security metadata exists.
[ ] Secret scan: no committed secrets.
[ ] Dependency scan: vulnerable auth/JWT libraries.
[ ] Test coverage: required negative tests present.
```

Non-automatable but review-required:

```text id="jfq5i0"
- business object policy correctness
- maker-checker semantics
- delegation/confused deputy logic
- risk acceptance
- audit evidence sufficiency
```

---

# 36. Minimal Production Invariants

```text id="9xoclm"
1. No unclassified endpoint.
2. No resource method parses JWT manually.
3. No tenant-scoped DB operation without tenant boundary.
4. No object ID from client without object-level authorization.
5. No sensitive mutation without state/version guard.
6. No internal endpoint accepting ordinary user token.
7. No permission/role/ACL change without audit and version increment.
8. No cached allow for sensitive action without fresh/version validation.
9. No security error response leaks token, SQL, stack trace, or tenant existence unintentionally.
10. No high-risk authz change merges without negative tests.
```

---

# 37. Roadmap Singkat

| Urutan | Fokus | Target |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Endpoint classification | Semua route punya boundary |
| 2 | AuthN validation | Token/API key/HMAC/mTLS benar |
| 3 | Principal model | Actor/tenant/delegation eksplisit |
| 4 | Permission policy | Permission catalog dan deny-by-default |
| 5 | Object/tenant review | BOLA/cross-tenant tertutup |
| 6 | MyBatis SQL review | SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE tenant-aware |
| 7 | Cache/revocation | Stale allow dicegah |
| 8 | Audit/error review | Forensic detail aman |
| 9 | Test review | Abuse cases jadi regression |
| 10 | Release gate | Tidak ada blocker sebelum go-live |

---

## Rekomendasi Materi Selanjutnya

Lanjutkan ke: **Cheatsheet Migration & Refactoring Legacy Authorization in Jersey/JAX-RS - replacing hardcoded roles, manual JWT parsing, unsafe mappers, missing tenant predicates, and scattered policies**.

---

**Sources:**

- [OWASP Application Security Verification Standard (ASVS)](https://owasp.org/www-project-application-security-verification-standard/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [Authorization - OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [V7 Error Handling and Logging - ASVS](https://github.com/OWASP/ASVS/blob/master/4.0/en/0x15-V7-Error-Logging.md?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/13/2026, 5:28:04 AM

# Cheatsheet - Migration & Refactoring Legacy Authorization di Jersey/JAX-RS

## 1. Tujuan

Cheatsheet ini fokus ke cara memigrasikan authorization legacy yang biasanya berbentuk:

```text id="2qpmy4"
- if (role == "ADMIN") tersebar di banyak service
- JWT parsing manual di resource method
- endpoint tanpa classification
- mapper MyBatis query by id tanpa tenant_id
- permission hardcoded tanpa catalog
- object-level authorization tidak konsisten
- cache permission tanpa revocation
- audit deny/action tidak lengkap
```

Target akhirnya:

```text id="0pxclo"
Authentication:
  centralized filter + token validator

Identity:
  SecurityActor / SecurityContext normalized

Authorization:
  permission catalog + policy class + decision object

Data access:
  tenant-aware mapper + state/version guard

Audit:
  actor/action/resource/decision/reason/policy version

Tests:
  negative regression for BOLA, tenant leak, stale permission, delegation
```

OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet menekankan least privilege, deny-by-default, dan validasi permission pada setiap request; refactoring legacy authorization harus mengarah ke tiga prinsip ini, bukan sekadar mengganti nama role menjadi permission. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Big Picture Migration

```mermaid id="m0ibh3"
flowchart TD
    A[Legacy Codebase] --> B[Inventory Endpoints]
    B --> C[Classify Public/User/Admin/Internal/Partner]
    C --> D[Find Manual JWT Parsing]
    D --> E[Introduce AuthenticationFilter + SecurityActor]
    E --> F[Build Permission Catalog]
    F --> G[Map Legacy Roles to Permissions]
    G --> H[Introduce Authorization Annotation]
    H --> I[Move Rules to Policy Classes]
    I --> J[Refactor MyBatis Queries Tenant-Aware]
    J --> K[Add Object-Level Authorization]
    K --> L[Add Audit Decision]
    L --> M[Add Revocation/Cache Version]
    M --> N[Add Regression Tests]
    N --> O[Remove Legacy Role Checks]
```

---

# 3. Migration Strategy

Jangan big-bang rewrite. Pakai strangler pattern.

```text id="c8oiuc"
Phase 1:
  inventory dan observability

Phase 2:
  introduce new security model tanpa memutus legacy

Phase 3:
  migrate endpoint per endpoint

Phase 4:
  enforce tests/gates

Phase 5:
  remove legacy bypass
```

Prinsip:

```text id="t53iqw"
No behavior change without test.
No new endpoint without new pattern.
No legacy bypass without migration ticket.
No role check without permission mapping.
No object ID without object-level policy.
```

---

# 4. Legacy Smell Catalog

| Smell | Risiko | Refactor Target |
|---|---|---|
| `@HeaderParam("Authorization")` di resource | token parsing tidak konsisten | `SecurityContext` |
| `Jwt.parse(...)` di resource/service | validasi bisa bypass | centralized token validator |
| `if role == ADMIN` | overprivilege/role explosion | permission + policy |
| `findById(id)` | BOLA/cross-tenant leak | `findByIdForTenant` |
| `UPDATE ... WHERE id=?` | cross-tenant mutation | `WHERE id AND tenant_id AND state/version` |
| endpoint tanpa annotation | accidental public | endpoint classification |
| `tenantId` dari body dipercaya | tenant spoofing | tenant from principal/mapping |
| permission string hardcoded | governance sulit | permission catalog |
| cache key `userId` saja | cross-tenant/stale | tenant+subject+version |
| audit hanya success | deny tidak forensic | decision audit |

OWASP API Security menjelaskan BOLA terjadi ketika attacker memanipulasi object ID di request target, header, query, atau payload; legacy `findById(id)` tanpa object authorization adalah pola yang harus diprioritaskan dalam migrasi. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 5. Inventory - Endpoint Discovery

Mulai dari daftar semua endpoint JAX-RS.

```text id="2xiz15"
Find:
  @Path classes
  @GET/@POST/@PUT/@PATCH/@DELETE methods
  @RolesAllowed
  custom annotations
  methods with @HeaderParam("Authorization")
  methods with @Context SecurityContext
```

Contoh script sederhana pakai grep/ripgrep:

```bash id="osabj5"
rg '@Path|@GET|@POST|@PUT|@PATCH|@DELETE|@RolesAllowed|Authorization|SecurityContext' src/main/java
```

Inventory table:

| Endpoint | Method | Current Guard | Classification | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/cases/{id}` | GET | role check in service | DOMAIN | BOLA risk |
| `/cases/{id}/approve` | POST | ADMIN role | DOMAIN | high |
| `/internal/reindex` | POST | none/VPN only | INTERNAL | critical |
| `/admin/users/{id}` | DELETE | ADMIN role | ADMIN | critical |
| `/webhooks/x` | POST | secret param | WEBHOOK | replay risk |

---

# 6. Inventory - Code Smell Search

## Manual JWT Parsing

```bash id="3oweko"
rg 'JWT|Jwt|Jwts|parseClaims|Authorization|Bearer' src/main/java
```

## Role Checks

```bash id="bxihwb"
rg 'hasRole|isUserInRole|ADMIN|SUPERUSER|RolesAllowed|role' src/main/java
```

## Unsafe Repository

```bash id="f4g8ud"
rg 'findById\\(|deleteById\\(|update.*ById|WHERE id =|where id =' src/main/resources src/main/java
```

## Tenant from Request

```bash id="yg743a"
rg 'tenantId|X-Tenant|tenant_id' src/main/java
```

Classify each finding:

```text id="0ebwb2"
KEEP:
  already safe

MIGRATE:
  needs refactor

BLOCK:
  security-critical unsafe pattern

SUPPRESS:
  false positive with justification
```

---

# 7. Build Compatibility Layer

Sebelum refactor semua endpoint, buat adapter yang bisa membaca legacy role tapi expose permission baru.

```java id="55czxl"
public interface PermissionResolver {
    Set<String> resolvePermissions(SecurityActor actor);
}
```

Legacy adapter:

```java id="t9ivsh"
public final class LegacyRolePermissionResolver implements PermissionResolver {

    private final Map<String, Set<String>> rolePermissionMapping;

    @Override
    public Set<String> resolvePermissions(SecurityActor actor) {
        Set<String> permissions = new HashSet<>(actor.permissions());

        for (String role : actor.roles()) {
            permissions.addAll(rolePermissionMapping.getOrDefault(role, Set.of()));
        }

        return Set.copyOf(permissions);
    }
}
```

Config:

```yaml id="4r8hlr"
legacyRolePermissionMapping:
  CASE_REVIEWER:
    - case.read
    - workflow.task.complete
  CASE_SUPERVISOR:
    - case.read.all
    - case.approve
  ADMIN:
    - admin.user.read
    - admin.user.disable
```

**Rule:** role-to-permission adapter hanya transisi. Jangan jadikan permanen tanpa catalog dan governance.

---

# 8. Introduce SecurityActor tanpa Mengubah Semua Resource

Step 1: centralized filter set `SecurityContext`.

Jakarta REST `ContainerRequestContext#setSecurityContext` dapat memasang security context baru untuk request saat ini, dan `SecurityContext#getUserPrincipal()` harus `null` jika request belum authenticated. Ini basis teknis untuk memindahkan parsing token dari resource ke filter. ([Jakarta EE](https://jakarta.ee/specifications/platform/10/apidocs/jakarta/ws/rs/container/containerrequestcontext?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

```java id="8l5ajx"
@Provider
@Priority(Priorities.AUTHENTICATION)
public class AuthenticationFilter implements ContainerRequestFilter {

    private final AccessTokenValidator validator;
    private final PrincipalMapper mapper;

    @Override
    public void filter(ContainerRequestContext ctx) {
        String token = extractBearer(ctx);
        VerifiedAccessToken verified = validator.validate(token);
        SecurityActor actor = mapper.map(verified, ctx);

        ctx.setSecurityContext(new RequestSecurityContext(
                actor,
                ctx.getSecurityContext() != null && ctx.getSecurityContext().isSecure(),
                "Bearer"
        ));
    }
}
```

Step 2: helper extraction.

```java id="y6ybg8"
public final class SecurityPrincipals {

    public static SecurityActor requireActor(SecurityContext ctx) {
        Principal principal = ctx.getUserPrincipal();

        if (!(principal instanceof SecurityActor actor)) {
            throw new NotAuthorizedException("Missing authenticated actor");
        }

        return actor;
    }
}
```

---

# 9. Refactor Pattern - Manual JWT Parsing

## Before

```java id="doju2h"
@GET
@Path("/cases/{caseId}")
public Response getCase(
        @HeaderParam("Authorization") String authorization,
        @PathParam("caseId") String caseId
) {
    Claims claims = JwtUtil.parse(authorization);
    String userId = claims.getSubject();
    String tenantId = claims.get("tenant_id", String.class);

    return Response.ok(caseService.getCase(userId, tenantId, caseId)).build();
}
```

## After

```java id="rprfb1"
@GET
@Path("/cases/{caseId}")
@RequiresPermission("case.read")
public Response getCase(
        @Context SecurityContext securityContext,
        @PathParam("caseId") String caseId
) {
    SecurityActor actor = SecurityPrincipals.requireActor(securityContext);

    return Response.ok(caseService.getCase(actor, caseId)).build();
}
```

Service:

```java id="ovr23x"
public CaseDto getCase(SecurityActor actor, String caseId) {
    CaseRecord record = caseRepository.findByIdForTenant(
            caseId,
            actor.tenantContext().tenantId()
    ).orElseThrow(() -> new NotFoundException("Case not found"));

    AuthorizationDecision decision = casePolicy.canRead(actor, record);

    auditLogger.logAuthorizationDecision(actor, "case.read", record, decision);

    if (!decision.allowed()) {
        throw new AccessDeniedException(decision.reason());
    }

    return CaseDto.from(record);
}
```

---

# 10. Refactor Pattern - `if ADMIN`

## Before

```java id="8c91xp"
if (!user.roles().contains("ADMIN")) {
    throw new ForbiddenException();
}
```

Problem:

```text id="j56dli"
ADMIN terlalu kasar.
Tidak jelas capability apa yang dibutuhkan.
Role berubah bisa memengaruhi banyak endpoint.
Tidak ada object/state policy.
```

## After

```java id="82931r"
AuthorizationDecision decision =
        authorizationService.requirePermission(actor, "admin.user.disable");

if (!decision.allowed()) {
    throw new AccessDeniedException(decision.reason());
}
```

Atau annotation:

```java id="bohcjd"
@DELETE
@Path("/admin/users/{userId}")
@AdminEndpoint(
        permissions = {"admin.user.disable"},
        requireStepUp = true
)
public Response disableUser(...) {
    ...
}
```

Policy:

```java id="plr5iv"
public AuthorizationDecision canDisableUser(
        SecurityActor actor,
        UserRecord targetUser
) {
    if (!actor.hasPermission("admin.user.disable")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_admin_user_disable");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(targetUser.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!actor.transportContext().strongAuthVerified()) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("step_up_required");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("admin_user_disable_allowed");
}
```

---

# 11. Refactor Pattern - `@RolesAllowed`

`@RolesAllowed` bisa tetap berguna untuk coarse compatibility, tetapi untuk domain authorization sebaiknya migrate ke permission.

## Before

```java id="6idxwf"
@RolesAllowed("ADMIN")
@POST
@Path("/cases/{caseId}/approve")
public Response approve(...) {
    ...
}
```

## Transitional

```java id="2k4ez6"
@RolesAllowed({"CASE_SUPERVISOR", "ADMIN"})
@RequiresPermission("case.approve")
@POST
@Path("/cases/{caseId}/approve")
public Response approve(...) {
    ...
}
```

## Final

```java id="cfn4e4"
@RequiresPermission("case.approve")
@POST
@Path("/cases/{caseId}/approve")
public Response approve(...) {
    ...
}
```

But final still needs object/state policy in service.

```text id="g52wgt"
Annotation checks capability.
Service policy checks object/state/tenant.
```

---

# 12. Refactor Pattern - Unsafe MyBatis `findById`

## Before

```java id="u74fha"
CaseRecord findById(@Param("id") String id);
```

```xml id="9t0avc"
<select id="findById" resultMap="CaseRecordMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM cases
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

## After

```java id="gnaxqr"
Optional<CaseRecord> findByIdForTenant(
        @Param("caseId") String caseId,
        @Param("tenantId") String tenantId
);
```

```xml id="zm9g22"
<select id="findByIdForTenant" resultMap="CaseRecordMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM cases
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
      AND tenant_id = #{tenantId}
</select>
```

Migration tactic:

```text id="2mk65b"
1. Add safe method.
2. Migrate callers one-by-one.
3. Deprecate unsafe method.
4. Add static scan forbidding unsafe method for tenant tables.
5. Delete unsafe method.
```

---

# 13. Refactor Pattern - Unsafe Update

## Before

```xml id="0t30xl"
<update id="approveCase">
    UPDATE cases
    SET status = 'APPROVED'
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
</update>
```

## After

```xml id="4hu284"
<update id="approveIfStillEligible">
    UPDATE cases
    SET status = 'APPROVED',
        approved_by = #{actorId},
        approved_at = now(),
        version = version + 1
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
      AND tenant_id = #{tenantId}
      AND status = 'SUBMITTED'
      AND created_by <> #{actorId}
      AND version = #{expectedVersion}
</update>
```

MyBatis dynamic SQL supports elements like `if`, `choose`, `where`, `set`, and `foreach`; during migration, be careful that dynamic branches never remove mandatory security predicates such as `tenant_id`. ([MyBatis](https://mybatis.org/mybatis-3/dynamic-sql.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 14. Refactor Pattern - Dynamic SQL with Unsafe Optional Tenant

## Before

```xml id="r8r62b"
<select id="searchCases" resultMap="CaseRecordMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM cases
    <where>
        <if test="tenantId != null">
            tenant_id = #{tenantId}
        </if>
        <if test="status != null">
            AND status = #{status}
        </if>
    </where>
</select>
```

Risk:

```text id="2il7ya"
tenantId null -> cross-tenant query.
```

## After

```xml id="9kexwy"
<select id="searchCases" resultMap="CaseRecordMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM cases
    WHERE tenant_id = #{tenantId}
    <if test="status != null">
        AND status = #{status}
    </if>
</select>
```

Java guard:

```java id="9hl7pk"
public SearchResult searchCases(SecurityActor actor, CaseSearchRequest request) {
    String tenantId = actor.tenantContext().tenantId();

    if (tenantId == null) {
        throw new AccessDeniedException("missing_tenant_context");
    }

    return mapper.searchCases(new CaseSearchCriteria(
            tenantId,
            request.status(),
            visibilityMode(actor)
    ));
}
```

---

# 15. Refactor Pattern - List/Search Visibility

## Before

```java id="gizfz9"
List<CaseRecord> cases = caseMapper.search(request);
return cases.stream()
        .filter(c -> canRead(actor, c))
        .toList();
```

Problem:

```text id="fo7v80"
Data already left DB.
Pagination/count can leak.
Performance poor.
```

## After

```java id="w2m2to"
CaseVisibilityCriteria criteria =
        CaseVisibilityCriteria.from(actor, request);

List<CaseRecord> cases = caseMapper.searchVisibleCases(criteria);
long total = caseMapper.countVisibleCases(criteria);
```

```xml id="jbpd31"
<select id="searchVisibleCases" resultMap="CaseRecordMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM cases c
    WHERE c.tenant_id = #{tenantId}
    <choose>
        <when test="visibilityMode == 'ALL'">
            AND 1 = 1
        </when>
        <when test="visibilityMode == 'ASSIGNED'">
            AND c.assigned_user_id = #{actorId}
        </when>
        <when test="visibilityMode == 'OWN'">
            AND c.created_by = #{actorId}
        </when>
        <when test="visibilityMode == 'ASSIGNED_OR_OWN'">
            AND (
                c.assigned_user_id = #{actorId}
                OR c.created_by = #{actorId}
            )
        </when>
        <otherwise>
            AND 1 = 0
        </otherwise>
    </choose>
</select>
```

---

# 16. Refactor Pattern - Public/Internal Endpoint Accident

## Before

```java id="vzczsx"
@Path("/internal/reindex")
public class ReindexResource {
    @POST
    public Response reindex(...) {
        ...
    }
}
```

No annotation, assumed protected by network.

## After

```java id="4jwij0"
@Path("/internal/reindex")
@InternalEndpoint(
        allowedServices = {"case-worker-service"},
        requiredPermissions = {"internal.case.reindex"},
        requireMtls = true
)
public class ReindexResource {
    @POST
    public Response reindex(@Context SecurityContext ctx, ReindexRequest request) {
        SecurityActor service = SecurityPrincipals.requireService(ctx);
        reindexService.reindex(service, request);
        return Response.accepted().build();
    }
}
```

Add route classification test:

```java id="cbio8c"
@Test
void everyJaxRsEndpointMustBeClassified() {
    List<ResourceMethod> unclassified =
            resourceScanner.findUnclassifiedJaxRsMethods();

    assertThat(unclassified).isEmpty();
}
```

---

# 17. Refactor Pattern - Scattered Policy

## Before

```java id="flqsk3"
if (user.hasRole("SUPERVISOR") && case.status().equals("SUBMITTED")) {
   ...
}
```

Repeated in several services.

## After

```java id="bcll4c"
public final class CaseApprovalPolicy {

    public AuthorizationDecision canApprove(
            SecurityActor actor,
            CaseRecord caseRecord,
            Set<String> freshPermissions
    ) {
        if (!freshPermissions.contains("case.approve")) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_case_approve");
        }

        if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(caseRecord.tenantId())) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("tenant_not_allowed");
        }

        if (caseRecord.status() != CaseStatus.SUBMITTED) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("case_not_submitted");
        }

        if (caseRecord.createdBy().equals(actor.effectiveActor().subject())) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("maker_checker_violation");
        }

        return AuthorizationDecision.allow("case_approve_allowed");
    }
}
```

Migration rule:

```text id="j4y01u"
One business action -> one policy method -> unit tests.
```

---

# 18. Refactor Pattern - Missing Audit

## Before

```java id="wk1r0p"
if (!policy.canApprove(actor, caseRecord)) {
    throw new ForbiddenException();
}
```

## After

```java id="gx7ant"
AuthorizationDecision decision =
        caseApprovalPolicy.canApprove(actor, caseRecord, freshPermissions);

auditLogger.logAuthorizationDecision(
        actor,
        "case.approve",
        ResourceRef.caseRef(caseRecord),
        decision
);

if (!decision.allowed()) {
    throw new AccessDeniedException(decision.reason());
}
```

Audit fields:

```text id="8217yx"
eventType
decision
reason
actor/effective actor/calling service
tenantId
action
resourceType/resourceId
policyName/policyVersion
correlationId
```

---

# 19. Migration Phases

## Phase 0 - Freeze New Legacy

Immediate rule:

```text id="hp58ee"
No new manual JWT parsing.
No new raw role check.
No new mapper by id for tenant tables.
No new endpoint without classification.
```

Add PR checklist before deep refactor.

---

## Phase 1 - Inventory and Risk Ranking

Rank endpoints by risk:

| Risk | Endpoint Type |
|---|---|
| Critical | admin, permission change, internal, partner money/order, bulk mutation |
| High | approve/delete/export/download |
| Medium | normal read/update |
| Low | public health/version |

Prioritize:

```text id="ksj4gb"
1. admin/internal/partner/webhook
2. state-changing endpoints
3. object read/download/export
4. list/search/count
5. low-risk reads
```

---

## Phase 2 - Platform Foundation

Build reusable components:

```text id="4z2cx9"
- AuthenticationFilter
- SecurityActor
- PrincipalMapper
- RequestSecurityContext
- @RequiresPermission
- @InternalEndpoint / @AdminEndpoint / @PublicEndpoint
- AuthorizationDecision
- SecurityAuditLogger
- Permission catalog
- EntitlementService
```

Do this before endpoint migration to reduce duplicated patterns.

---

## Phase 3 - Endpoint-by-Endpoint Migration

For each endpoint:

```text id="t8srb0"
1. classify endpoint
2. add annotation
3. replace JWT parsing with SecurityActor
4. replace role check with permission
5. add object policy
6. refactor mapper tenant/status guard
7. add audit
8. add negative tests
9. remove old code path
```

---

## Phase 4 - Tighten Enforcement

After enough migration:

```text id="oxgr86"
- fail build on unclassified endpoint
- forbid manual Authorization parsing in resource
- forbid unsafe mapper methods
- remove broad role fallback
- require permission catalog entry
- enforce route/security OpenAPI metadata
```

---

## Phase 5 - Remove Legacy Compatibility

Remove:

```text id="p0xkqw"
- LegacyRolePermissionResolver if no longer needed
- @RolesAllowed-only protections
- unsafe findById methods
- old JWT util exposed to resources
- deprecated role names
- bypass flags
```

---

# 20. Migration Backlog Template

```text id="ltxnh9"
Migration item:
Endpoint/class:
Current pattern:
Risk:
Target pattern:
Required permission:
Tenant source:
Object policy:
Mapper changes:
Audit changes:
Tests:
Owner:
Status:
```

Example:

```text id="ap1em9"
Migration item:
  CASE-APPROVE-AUTHZ

Endpoint:
  POST /cases/{caseId}/approve

Current:
  @RolesAllowed("ADMIN"), mapper update by id

Risk:
  Critical

Target:
  @RequiresPermission("case.approve")
  CaseApprovalPolicy
  approveIfStillEligible SQL

Tests:
  missing permission
  wrong tenant
  maker-checker
  invalid status
  stale version
```

---

# 21. Permission Migration Table

Map old roles to new permissions.

| Legacy Role | New Permissions | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CASE_REVIEWER` | `case.read`, `workflow.task.complete` | no approve |
| `CASE_SUPERVISOR` | `case.read.all`, `case.approve`, `case.assign` | tenant-scoped |
| `TENANT_ADMIN` | `admin.user.read`, `admin.user.disable`, `admin.role.assign` | step-up for assign |
| `SYSTEM_WORKER` | `internal.case.reindex`, `internal.workflow.timeout.handle` | service only |
| `SUPER_ADMIN` | avoid direct mapping | split into specific admin permissions |

Rule:

```text id="s6zj2n"
Do not map SUPER_ADMIN to every permission blindly.
Use migration as chance to split least privilege.
```

---

# 22. Feature Flag Migration

Use feature flag to compare legacy vs new decision.

```java id="u3r8os"
AuthorizationDecision legacyDecision =
        legacyAuthorization.canApprove(actor, caseRecord);

AuthorizationDecision newDecision =
        newAuthorization.canApprove(actor, caseRecord);

decisionComparisonLogger.log(
        actor,
        "case.approve",
        ResourceRef.caseRef(caseRecord),
        legacyDecision,
        newDecision
);

if (featureFlags.useNewAuthorization("case.approve")) {
    enforce(newDecision);
} else {
    enforce(legacyDecision);
}
```

Use shadow mode:

```text id="jip5fg"
legacy enforced
new evaluated and logged

Then:
new enforced
legacy removed
```

Alert if disagreement:

```text id="a0ee8y"
legacy allow + new deny:
  potential privilege reduction; review

legacy deny + new allow:
  potential privilege expansion; block unless intentional
```

---

# 23. Shadow Decision Audit

```json id="z2rqrv"
{
  "eventType": "AUTHORIZATION_SHADOW_DECISION",
  "action": "case.approve",
  "resourceType": "case",
  "resourceId": "CASE-789",
  "legacyDecision": "ALLOW",
  "legacyReason": "role_admin",
  "newDecision": "DENY",
  "newReason": "maker_checker_violation",
  "migrationFlag": "case.approve.newAuthz",
  "correlationId": "req-123"
}
```

---

# 24. Compatibility Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Users lose access unexpectedly | shadow compare + migration report |
| Users gain access unexpectedly | block legacy deny/new allow |
| Role mapping incomplete | permission diff report |
| Performance regression | cache/versioned entitlement |
| Audit volume spike | dedicated event type/sampling for shadow |
| Endpoint missed | route classification test |
| Query missed | static mapper scan |
| Business policy misunderstood | feature owner review |

# 26. Static Guardrails

## Forbid Authorization Header in Resource

Test idea:

```java id="z6loco"
@Test
void resourcesMustNotInjectAuthorizationHeader() {
    List<String> offenders = sourceScanner.findMethodsContaining(
            "@HeaderParam(\"Authorization\")"
    );

    assertThat(offenders).isEmpty();
}
```

## Forbid Unsafe Mapper Methods

```java id="c813fo"
@Test
void tenantScopedMappersMustNotExposeFindByIdOnly() {
    List<String> offenders = mapperScanner.findMethodsMatching(
            "findById\\("
    );

    assertThat(offenders)
            .doesNotContainAnyElementsOf(tenantScopedMapperMethods());
}
```

## Route Classification

```java id="wgnwpu"
@Test
void everyEndpointMustBeClassified() {
    assertThat(routeScanner.unclassifiedEndpoints()).isEmpty();
}
```

---

# 27. OpenRewrite / Automated Refactor Idea

For Java codebase, automated refactoring can help but must be reviewed.

Possible recipes:

```text id="xe90m9"
- flag @HeaderParam("Authorization") in @Path classes
- replace JwtUtil.parse in resource with SecurityActor helper stub
- add @RequiresPermission TODO annotation
- mark mapper findById as @Deprecated
- insert route classification TODO
```

Do not blindly auto-migrate business authorization. Object/state policy needs human review.

---

# 28. Regression Test Pack per Migrated Endpoint

For each migrated endpoint, require:

```text id="h2v1lf"
AuthN:
  missing/invalid token

AuthZ:
  valid token missing permission

Object:
  same tenant but not owner/assigned/ACL

Tenant:
  cross-tenant object

State:
  invalid workflow/status

Mutation:
  stale version/update count zero

Audit:
  deny and allow event exists

Error:
  401/403/404/409 semantics
```

For BOLA-prone endpoints, OWASP explicitly highlights object ID manipulation in paths/query/header/payload; test must include attacker-controlled ID scenarios, not only role checks. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

Track progress:

```text id="qz8iay"
Total endpoints:
Classified endpoints:

Endpoints using @RolesAllowed only:

Object policies added:

High-risk blockers:
```

Example table:

| Metric | Count | Target |
|---|---:|---:|
| JAX-RS endpoints | 180 | 180 |
| Classified | 120 | 180 |
| Manual JWT parsing | 18 | 0 |
| Unsafe `findById` | 31 | 0 |
| `@RolesAllowed` only | 42 | 0 |

---

# 30. Rollout Plan

```text id="j5rqpj"
1. Merge foundation with no behavior change.
2. Enable audit/shadow decision.
3. Migrate low-risk endpoint to validate pattern.
4. Migrate high-risk admin/internal/partner endpoints.
5. Migrate read endpoints with object policy.
6. Migrate mutation endpoints with SQL guards.
7. Enable CI gates.
8. Remove legacy compatibility.
```

Avoid:

```text id="b5coel"
Migrate everything in one PR.
Change permission model and business behavior without shadow/testing.
Remove old roles before permission mapping validated.
```

---

# 31. Rollback Strategy

For each migrated endpoint:

```text id="1h6ga1"
- feature flag for new authz enforcement
- shadow logging enabled
- previous behavior known
- DB migration reversible or additive
- permission catalog additive first
- old mapper method not deleted until endpoint stable
```

Rollback example:

```text id="pc0rd2"
If new policy denies too many legitimate approvals:
  disable feature flag
  keep audit/shadow decision
  analyze mismatch
  fix policy
  re-enable
```

Do not rollback by disabling all authorization.

---

# 32. Data Migration Risks

| Change | Risk | Safer Migration |
|---|---|---|
| Add permission catalog | missing permission | additive insert |
| Map roles to permissions | access changes | shadow compare |
| Add tenant_id required | old rows null | backfill + constraint later |
| Add revoked_at | active logic bug | dual query tests |
| Add version field | cache mismatch | default version=1 |
| Remove role | users lose access | usage report first |
| Split admin role | privilege gaps | staged mapping |

---

# 33. Legacy Audit Gap Remediation

If old system did not audit enough:

```text id="rqahmk"
1. Start auditing new decisions immediately.
2. Add audit to legacy path before full migration if needed.
3. For critical admin/permission changes, backfill from DB history if available.
4. Mark historical audit gaps honestly.
5. Add monitoring for high-risk endpoint access.
```

Do not fabricate historical audit evidence.

---

# 34. Communication Plan

Authorization migration changes behavior. Communicate:

```text id="a6n82s"
- permission catalog changes
- role-to-permission mapping
- admin/user impact
- partner/service credential changes
- expected 403 increases
- new error semantics
- rollout schedule
- support escalation path
```

For support/admin teams:

```text id="klm5fl"
Before:
  user needs ADMIN

After:
  user needs case.approve and object must be submitted/assigned/tenant-valid
```

---

# 35. Common Migration Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Risiko | Perbaikan |
|---|---|---|
| Big-bang rewrite | outage/access regression | phased migration |
| Rename roles to permissions only | no real least privilege | split capability model |
| Keep ADMIN fallback forever | migration never ends | expiry/deprecation plan |
| Add annotation but no object policy | BOLA remains | service-layer policy |
| Query all then filter | leak/perf bug | visibility SQL |
| No shadow compare | unknown behavior change | shadow decision |
| No negative tests | regressions | required test pack |
| Delete old code too early | rollback hard | staged removal |
| No audit during migration | blind incidents | audit first |
| Ignoring business owners | wrong policy | review with domain owner |

---

# 36. Migration Checklist - Compact

```text id="xfuiia"
[ ] Endpoint inventory complete.
[ ] Risk ranking complete.
[ ] New auth foundation merged.
[ ] Permission catalog created.
[ ] Legacy role-to-permission mapping drafted.
[ ] Route classification annotations added.
[ ] Manual JWT parsing removed from migrated endpoint.
[ ] Role check replaced with permission/policy.
[ ] Object-level policy implemented.
[ ] MyBatis query/update tenant-aware.
[ ] Audit decision emitted.
[ ] Cache/revocation handled.
[ ] Negative tests added.
[ ] Shadow decision compared if behavior risky.
[ ] Legacy code path deprecated/removed.
```

---

# 37. Definition of Done for a Migrated Endpoint

```text id="vg5ff1"
A migrated endpoint is done when:

1. It has explicit classification.
2. It uses centralized authentication.
3. Resource method uses SecurityActor.
4. Required permission/scope is declared.
5. Application service enforces object/tenant/state policy.
6. MyBatis queries/mutations are tenant/state/version guarded.
7. Authorization decision is audited.
8. Error semantics are correct.
9. Negative tests pass.
10. Legacy bypass is removed or feature-flagged with expiry.
```

---

# 38. Minimal Production Invariants

```text id="oa9iy1"
1. New code must not add legacy authorization patterns.
2. Manual JWT parsing must move to authentication filter.
3. Role checks must migrate to permission plus policy.
4. Object IDs must be protected by object-level authorization.
5. Tenant-scoped data access must include tenant boundary in SQL.
6. Sensitive mutations must include state/version guard.
7. Migration must include negative tests, not only happy path.
8. Legacy/new decision differences must be logged during risky rollout.
9. Audit should be added before or during migration, not after.
10. Legacy fallback must have a removal plan.
```

---

# 39. Roadmap Singkat

| Urutan | Fokus | Target |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Inventory | Semua endpoint/mapper/role smell terlihat |
| 2 | Freeze legacy | Tidak ada pola legacy baru |
| 3 | Foundation | SecurityActor, filters, annotations, audit |
| 4 | Permission catalog | Role lama dipetakan ke capability |
| 5 | Endpoint migration | Per endpoint, bukan big bang |
| 6 | Mapper hardening | Tenant/status/version guard |
| 7 | Shadow compare | Behavior change terukur |
| 8 | CI guardrails | Legacy pattern gagal build |
| 9 | Cleanup | Remove old JWT utils/role fallback |
| 10 | Governance | Migration dashboard + release gate |
