**Sources:**

- [API1:2023 Broken Object Level Authorization](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [Documentation: 18: 5.5. Constraints](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-constraints.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/13/2026, 5:39:07 AM

# Cheatsheet - Workflow/Camunda Authorization Deep Dive di Jersey/JAX-RS

## 1. Tujuan

Cheatsheet ini fokus ke authorization untuk workflow/BPMN/Camunda ketika aplikasi Jersey/JAX-RS expose endpoint seperti:

```text
GET  /tasks
GET  /tasks/{taskId}
POST /tasks/{taskId}/claim
POST /tasks/{taskId}/complete
POST /tasks/{taskId}/assign
POST /tasks/{taskId}/escalate
POST /processes/{definitionKey}/start
POST /process-instances/{id}/cancel
```

Masalah yang ingin dicegah:

```text
- User complete task hanya bermodal taskId.
- Task tenant lain bisa dibaca/complete.
- Candidate group hanya dianggap UI filter, bukan security guard.
- User bisa complete task yang bukan assignee/candidate.
- User bisa approve object yang dia buat sendiri.
- Service worker memakai privilege internal untuk bypass original user.
- Process instance dibatalkan tanpa ownership/tenant/business authorization.
- Camunda task authorization tidak sinkron dengan authorization domain aplikasi.
```

Prinsip dasarnya: authorization workflow tidak boleh hanya bergantung pada task ID, role global, atau UI Tasklist. OWASP Authorization Cheat Sheet menekankan least privilege, deny-by-default, dan permission validation pada setiap request; ini harus diterapkan juga pada endpoint workflow yang memanggil Camunda. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Mental Model

```text
Can <actor> perform <workflow action>
on <task/process instance>
for <business object>
within <tenant>
under <assignment/candidate/state/policy>?
```

Workflow authorization minimal harus mengecek:

| Dimensi | Contoh |
|---|---|
| Actor | user, service worker, admin, delegated service |
| Tenant | task tenant, process tenant, business object tenant |
| Workflow resource | taskId, processInstanceId, processDefinitionKey |
| Business resource | caseId, orderId, customerId |
| Assignment | assignee, candidate user, candidate group |
| Permission | `workflow.task.complete`, `case.approve` |
| State | task active, process active, case `SUBMITTED` |
| Separation of duty | maker-checker, four-eyes approval |
| Audit | actor, taskId, processInstanceId, businessKey, decision |

**Rule utama:**

```text
Camunda task authorization is not enough if the business action also changes domain data.
Business authorization is not enough if the workflow task assignment is ignored.
You usually need both.
```

---

# 3. Camunda 7 vs Camunda 8 - Authorization Mindset

| Area | Camunda 7 | Camunda 8 |
|---|---|---|
| Engine model | Process engine API / embedded or REST | Zeebe + Orchestration Cluster APIs/components |
| User task model | assignee, candidate user/group via task service | user tasks with assignee/candidateUsers/candidateGroups |
| Authorization style | Engine authorization service if untrusted users access engine API directly | Orchestration Cluster authorization for Zeebe/Admin/Operate/Tasklist/API |
| Multi-tenancy | tenant ID support, transparent access restriction via authentication | tenant identifiers in a single installation; self-managed multi-tenancy |
| App responsibility | if embedded and app controls engine API, app can enforce query/command restrictions | app still must enforce business/object/tenant rules around its API |

Camunda 7 docs explicitly distinguish between embedded engine usage where the application controls engine API access, versus exposing Camunda REST/Webapp to users; if untrusted users can directly construct process engine queries/commands, Camunda authorization needs to be enabled. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/user-guide/process-engine/authorization-service/))

Camunda 8 Orchestration Cluster authorization is described as fine-grained access control for Zeebe, Admin, Operate, Tasklist, and Orchestration Cluster APIs; when enabled, no access is granted by default and permissions must be explicitly assigned. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/access-control/authorizations/))

---

# 4. Big Picture Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A[Client / Task UI / Service] --> B[Jersey Resource]
    B --> C[Authentication Filter]
    C --> D[SecurityActor + TenantContext]
    D --> E[Endpoint Permission Filter]

    E --> F[Workflow Application Service]
    F --> G[Load Task / Process Metadata]
    G --> H[Load Business Object by Tenant]
    H --> I[Workflow Authorization Policy]
    I --> J{Decision}

    J -->|Deny| K[403/404/409 + Audit]
    J -->|Allow| L[Camunda Command]
    L --> M[Complete / Claim / Assign / Start / Cancel]

    M --> N[Domain DB Update / Outbox]
    N --> O[Audit Business Event]

    G --> P[Camunda 7 Engine API or Camunda 8 APIs]
    H --> Q[MyBatis/PostgreSQL]
```

---

# 5. Core Invariant

```text
Every workflow command must bind together:

1. authenticated actor
2. tenant context
3. Camunda task/process resource
4. business resource
5. assignment/candidate/group relation
6. domain permission
7. domain state rule
8. audit decision
```

Jangan pernah:

```text
taskService.complete(taskId)
```

tanpa mengecek:

```text
- task exists and is active
- task tenant matches actor tenant
- task belongs to expected process/business key
- actor is assignee/candidate/supervisor/admin
- actor has required permission
- business object is visible and eligible
```

---

# 6. User Task Concepts

Camunda 7 user task assignment mendukung `assignee`, `candidateUsers`, dan `candidateGroups`. Dokumentasi Camunda 7 menjelaskan bahwa assignee adalah satu user yang ditugaskan ke task, sedangkan candidate user/group dipakai untuk potential owner; candidate group membership dapat di-resolve melalui IdentityService. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/reference/bpmn20/tasks/user-task/))

Camunda 8 user task model juga punya `assignee`, `candidateUsers`, dan `candidateGroups`; dokumentasi Camunda 8.9 mencatat bahwa usernames dan group IDs bersifat case-sensitive, dan sejak Camunda 8.8 candidate groups sebaiknya mereferensikan group IDs. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/modeler/bpmn/user-tasks/))

**Practical warning:**

```text
Assignee/candidate metadata is not automatically equal to business authorization.
It tells who may work on the task, but domain policy still decides whether the business action is allowed.
```

---

# 7. Camunda 8 Tasklist V2 Nuance

Untuk Camunda 8.9 Tasklist V2 dengan authorization enabled, dokumentasi Tasklist merekomendasikan kombinasi process-level permissions dan task-level permissions; Tasklist mengevaluasi `PROCESS_DEFINITION` permissions dan `USER_TASK` permissions, termasuk property-based access control pada `assignee`, `candidateUsers`, dan `candidateGroups`. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/tasklist/user-task-authorization/))

Namun dokumentasi user task model juga menekankan perbedaan antara assignment metadata dan access restriction model; jangan mengasumsikan BPMN `candidateGroups` saja akan mengamankan custom Jersey API Anda. Custom API tetap harus melakukan authorization sendiri sebelum memanggil Camunda command. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/modeler/bpmn/user-tasks/))

---

# 8. Workflow Permission Catalog

Gunakan permission terpisah untuk workflow action.

```text
workflow.task.read
workflow.task.claim
workflow.task.unclaim
workflow.task.assign
workflow.task.reassign
workflow.task.complete
workflow.task.escalate
workflow.task.delegate
workflow.process.start
workflow.process.cancel
workflow.process.suspend
workflow.process.retry-job
workflow.incident.resolve
```

Domain permission tetap terpisah:

```text
case.read
case.approve
case.reject
case.return
case.reopen
order.submit
order.cancel
```

Mapping action:

| Endpoint | Workflow Permission | Domain Permission |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /tasks` | `workflow.task.read` | object visibility permission |
| `POST /tasks/{id}/claim` | `workflow.task.claim` | optional domain read |
| `POST /tasks/{id}/complete` | `workflow.task.complete` | e.g. `case.approve` |
| `POST /tasks/{id}/assign` | `workflow.task.assign` | maybe supervisor permission |
| `POST /processes/{key}/start` | `workflow.process.start` | e.g. `case.create` |
| `POST /process-instances/{id}/cancel` | `workflow.process.cancel` | domain cancel/admin permission |

---

# 9. Resource Model

Buat abstraction agar service tidak langsung bergantung ke detail Camunda 7/8.

```java
public record WorkflowTaskRef(
        String taskId,
        String processInstanceId,
        String processDefinitionKey,
        String taskDefinitionKey,
        String taskName,
        String tenantId,
        String businessKey,
        String businessResourceType,
        String businessResourceId,
        String assignee,
        Set<String> candidateUsers,
        Set<String> candidateGroups,
        boolean active
) {}
```

```java
public interface WorkflowTaskGateway {

    Optional<WorkflowTaskRef> findActiveTaskById(String taskId);

    List<WorkflowTaskRef> searchVisibleTasks(TaskSearchCriteria criteria);

    void claimTask(String taskId, String actorId);

    void unclaimTask(String taskId);

    void assignTask(String taskId, String assignee);

    void completeTask(String taskId, Map<String, Object> variables);
}
```

Implementasi bisa berbeda:

```text
Camunda7WorkflowTaskGateway
Camunda8WorkflowTaskGateway
```

Service bisnis tidak perlu tahu apakah backend Camunda 7 atau Camunda 8.

---

# 10. Task Authorization Policy

```java
public final class WorkflowTaskAuthorizationPolicy {

    public AuthorizationDecision canReadTask(
            SecurityActor actor,
            WorkflowTaskRef task,
            BusinessObjectRef businessObject
    ) {
        if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(task.tenantId())) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_tenant_not_allowed");
        }

        if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.task.read")) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_task_read");
        }

        if (isAssignedToActor(actor, task)) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_assigned_to_actor");
        }

        if (isCandidateActor(actor, task)) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_candidate_actor");
        }

        if (actor.hasPermission("workflow.task.read.all")) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_read_all");
        }

        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("not_task_assignee_or_candidate");
    }

    public AuthorizationDecision canCompleteTask(
            SecurityActor actor,
            WorkflowTaskRef task,
            BusinessObjectRef businessObject
    ) {
        if (!task.active()) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_not_active");
        }

        if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(task.tenantId())) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_tenant_not_allowed");
        }

        if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.task.complete")) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_task_complete");
        }

        if (isAssignedToActor(actor, task)) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_complete_assignee");
        }

        if (task.assignee() == null && isCandidateActor(actor, task)) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_complete_candidate");
        }

        if (actor.hasPermission("workflow.task.complete.any")) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_complete_any");
        }

        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("not_allowed_to_complete_task");
    }

    private boolean isAssignedToActor(SecurityActor actor, WorkflowTaskRef task) {
        return task.assignee() != null
                && task.assignee().equals(actor.effectiveActor().subject());
    }

    private boolean isCandidateActor(SecurityActor actor, WorkflowTaskRef task) {
        String subject = actor.effectiveActor().subject();

        if (task.candidateUsers().contains(subject)) {
            return true;
        }

        return actor.groups().stream().anyMatch(task.candidateGroups()::contains);
    }
}
```

**Important:** `workflow.task.complete.any` harus sangat terbatas, biasanya supervisor/admin dengan audit ketat.

---

# 11. Business Policy + Task Policy

Untuk approval, jangan hanya cek task assignment.

```java
public final class CaseApprovalPolicy {

    public AuthorizationDecision canApproveCase(
            SecurityActor actor,
            CaseRecord caseRecord
    ) {
        if (!actor.hasPermission("case.approve")) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_case_approve");
        }

        if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(caseRecord.tenantId())) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("case_tenant_not_allowed");
        }

        if (caseRecord.status() != CaseStatus.SUBMITTED) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("case_not_submitted");
        }

        if (caseRecord.createdBy().equals(actor.effectiveActor().subject())) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("maker_checker_violation");
        }

        if (actor.approvalLevel() < caseRecord.requiredApprovalLevel()) {
            return AuthorizationDecision.deny("approval_level_too_low");
        }

        return AuthorizationDecision.allow("case_approval_allowed");
    }
}
```

Completion allowed jika:

```text
workflow task policy allows complete
AND
business policy allows the domain action
```

---

# 12. Complete Task Flow

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant API as Jersey API
    participant Svc as WorkflowApplicationService
    participant Camunda as Camunda Gateway
    participant DB as PostgreSQL/MyBatis
    participant Audit

    Client->>API: POST /tasks/{taskId}/complete
    API->>Svc: completeTask(actor, taskId, payload)
    Svc->>Camunda: findActiveTaskById(taskId)
    Camunda-->>Svc: WorkflowTaskRef
    Svc->>DB: find business object by tenant + businessResourceId
    DB-->>Svc: CaseRecord
    Svc->>Svc: task policy canComplete
    Svc->>Svc: business policy canApprove/reject/etc
    Svc->>Audit: AUTHORIZATION_DECISION
    Svc->>Camunda: completeTask(taskId, variables)
    Svc->>DB: update domain state / outbox
    Svc->>Audit: WORKFLOW_TASK_COMPLETED
    Svc-->>API: 204
```

---

# 13. Complete Task Service Pattern

```java
public final class WorkflowApplicationService {

    private final WorkflowTaskGateway workflowTaskGateway;
    private final CaseRepository caseRepository;
    private final WorkflowTaskAuthorizationPolicy taskPolicy;
    private final CaseApprovalPolicy caseApprovalPolicy;
    private final SecurityAuditLogger auditLogger;

    @Transactional
    public void completeApprovalTask(
            SecurityActor actor,
            String taskId,
            CompleteApprovalRequest request
    ) {
        WorkflowTaskRef task = workflowTaskGateway.findActiveTaskById(taskId)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new NotFoundException("Task not found"));

        CaseRecord caseRecord = caseRepository.findByIdForTenant(
                task.tenantId(),
                task.businessResourceId()
        ).orElseThrow(() -> new NotFoundException("Case not found"));

        AuthorizationDecision taskDecision =
                taskPolicy.canCompleteTask(actor, task, BusinessObjectRef.caseRef(caseRecord));

        AuthorizationDecision caseDecision =
                caseApprovalPolicy.canApproveCase(actor, caseRecord);

        AuthorizationDecision finalDecision =
                AuthorizationDecision.allOf(taskDecision, caseDecision);

        auditLogger.logWorkflowAuthorizationDecision(
                actor,
                "workflow.task.complete",
                task,
                caseRecord,
                finalDecision
        );

        if (!finalDecision.allowed()) {
            throw new AccessDeniedException(finalDecision.reason());
        }

        Map<String, Object> variables = Map.of(
                "approvalDecision", request.decision(),
                "approvedBy", actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
                "approvalComment", request.comment()
        );

        workflowTaskGateway.completeTask(task.taskId(), variables);

        caseRepository.markApprovalSubmitted(
                caseRecord.tenantId(),
                caseRecord.id(),
                actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
                caseRecord.version()
        );

        auditLogger.logWorkflowTaskCompleted(actor, task, caseRecord);
    }
}
```

**Caveat transaksi:** Camunda command dan domain DB update mungkin tidak berada dalam satu transaksi yang sama, terutama pada Camunda 8/Zeebe atau remote engine. Untuk production, gunakan outbox/saga/idempotency agar tidak terjadi inconsistent state.

---

# 14. Camunda 7 Embedded Engine Pattern

Camunda 7 docs menyebut jika application sepenuhnya mengontrol API method yang dipanggil ke process engine, application dapat membatasi query/command, misalnya task query dibatasi ke assignee tertentu. Tetapi jika Camunda REST API langsung diekspos ke user, authorization Camunda perlu dinyalakan agar user tidak bisa mengirim query bebas. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/user-guide/process-engine/authorization-service/))

Pattern untuk embedded engine:

```java
public List<Task> findVisibleTasks(SecurityActor actor) {
    return taskService.createTaskQuery()
            .taskTenantIdIn(actor.tenantContext().requireCurrentTenant())
            .or()
                .taskAssignee(actor.effectiveActor().subject())
                .taskCandidateUser(actor.effectiveActor().subject())
            .endOr()
            .active()
            .list();
}
```

Jika memakai Camunda 7 tenant transparent access, dokumentasi menunjukkan `identityService.setAuthentication(user, groups, tenantIds)` dapat membuat API calls transparently restricted ke tenant ID; contoh `taskService.complete(...)` akan melempar exception jika task tenant berbeda dari tenant authentication. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/user-guide/process-engine/multi-tenancy/))

---

# 15. Camunda 7 `setAuthentication` Guard

```java
public <T> T withCamundaAuthentication(SecurityActor actor, Supplier<T> work) {
    List<String> groups = new ArrayList<>(actor.groups());
    List<String> tenantIds = List.of(actor.tenantContext().requireCurrentTenant());

    try {
        identityService.setAuthentication(
                actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
                groups,
                tenantIds
        );

        return work.get();

    } finally {
        identityService.clearAuthentication();
    }
}
```

Usage:

```java
public void completeTaskCamunda7(SecurityActor actor, String taskId, Map<String, Object> vars) {
    withCamundaAuthentication(actor, () -> {
        taskService.complete(taskId, vars);
        return null;
    });
}
```

**Important:**

```text
Always clear authentication in finally.
Do not leak Camunda authentication across pooled threads.
Do not rely only on this if business policy is more specific.
```

---

# 16. Camunda 8 Multi-Tenancy Consideration

Camunda 8 multi-tenancy is supported for Self-Managed; the docs state it uses tenant identifiers in a single installation, appending tenant identifiers to data objects such as process definitions, process instances, and jobs. It also states multi-tenancy checks are disabled by default and must be enabled to restrict access based on tenant assignments. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/multi-tenancy/))

For Jersey application code:

```text
Do not assume Camunda tenant checks are enabled.
Do not assume your domain DB tenant matches Camunda tenant unless you validate it.
```

Recommended invariant:

```text
task.tenantId == businessObject.tenantId == actor.currentTenantId
```

---

# 17. Task Claim Policy

Claiming a task is not just assignment update.

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canClaimTask(
        SecurityActor actor,
        WorkflowTaskRef task
) {
    if (!task.active()) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_not_active");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(task.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.task.claim")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_task_claim");
    }

    if (task.assignee() != null) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_already_assigned");
    }

    if (task.candidateUsers().contains(actor.effectiveActor().subject())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.allow("candidate_user_can_claim");
    }

    if (actor.groups().stream().anyMatch(task.candidateGroups()::contains)) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.allow("candidate_group_can_claim");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.deny("not_task_candidate");
}
```

Endpoint:

```java
@POST
@Path("/tasks/{taskId}/claim")
@RequiresPermission("workflow.task.claim")
public Response claim(
        @Context SecurityContext securityContext,
        @PathParam("taskId") String taskId
) {
    SecurityActor actor = SecurityPrincipals.requireActor(securityContext);
    workflowApplicationService.claimTask(actor, taskId);
    return Response.noContent().build();
}
```

---

# 18. Reassign / Delegate / Escalate Policy

Reassignment is high-risk because it changes who can act.

| Action | Required Permission | Extra Guard |
|---|---|---|
| claim | `workflow.task.claim` | actor is candidate |
| unclaim self | `workflow.task.unclaim` | actor is assignee |
| assign self | `workflow.task.claim` | candidate/eligible |
| assign other | `workflow.task.assign` | supervisor/admin + same tenant |
| reassign | `workflow.task.reassign` | old/new assignee valid |
| delegate | `workflow.task.delegate` | delegated user eligible |
| escalate | `workflow.task.escalate` | SLA/state + supervisor group |

Reassign policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canReassignTask(
        SecurityActor actor,
        WorkflowTaskRef task,
        String newAssignee
) {
    if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.task.reassign")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_task_reassign");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(task.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("task_tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!userDirectory.isUserInTenant(newAssignee, task.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("new_assignee_not_in_tenant");
    }

    if (!supervisorPolicy.canManageActor(actor, task.assignee(), task.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("not_supervisor_of_assignee");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("task_reassign_allowed");
}
```

---

# 19. Start Process Authorization

Starting a process creates business consequences.

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canStartProcess(
        SecurityActor actor,
        String processDefinitionKey,
        String targetTenantId,
        StartProcessRequest request
) {
    if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.process.start")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_process_start");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(targetTenantId)) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!processCatalog.isStartAllowed(processDefinitionKey, actor, targetTenantId)) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("process_definition_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!domainPolicy.canCreateBusinessObject(actor, request.businessType())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("domain_create_not_allowed");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("process_start_allowed");
}
```

Start process should set:

```text
tenantId
businessKey
initiator
correlationId
domain resource ID
```

Variables example:

```java
Map<String, Object> variables = Map.of(
        "tenantId", actor.tenantContext().requireCurrentTenant(),
        "initiatorUserId", actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
        "businessResourceType", "case",
        "businessResourceId", caseId,
        "correlationId", correlationId
);
```

---

# 20. Cancel / Suspend Process Instance

Canceling a process can destroy work or bypass approvals.

Policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canCancelProcessInstance(
        SecurityActor actor,
        WorkflowProcessInstanceRef instance,
        BusinessObjectRef businessObject
) {
    if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.process.cancel")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_process_cancel");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(instance.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("process_tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!businessObject.tenantId().equals(instance.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("process_business_tenant_mismatch");
    }

    if (!actor.hasPermission("case.cancel.workflow")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_domain_workflow_cancel");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("process_cancel_allowed");
}
```

For admin/platform cancel:

```text
require step-up
require reason
audit old/new state
maybe require approval
```

---

# 21. Maker-Checker / Four-Eyes in Workflow

Common approval rule:

```text
Creator/requester must not approve own request.
Reviewer and final approver must be different users.
High-risk amount needs two approvals.
```

Schema example:

```sql
CREATE TABLE case_approval_step (
    tenant_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
    case_id        TEXT NOT NULL,
    step_no        INT NOT NULL,
    approver_id    TEXT NOT NULL,
    decision       TEXT NOT NULL,
    decided_at     TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, case_id, step_no)
);
```

Policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canGiveSecondApproval(
        SecurityActor actor,
        CaseRecord caseRecord,
        List<ApprovalStep> previousApprovals
) {
    String actorId = actor.effectiveActor().subject();

    if (caseRecord.createdBy().equals(actorId)) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("creator_cannot_approve");
    }

    boolean alreadyApproved = previousApprovals.stream()
            .anyMatch(step -> step.approverId().equals(actorId));

    if (alreadyApproved) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("same_user_cannot_approve_twice");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("second_approval_allowed");
}
```

---

# 22. Task Variables Security

Task completion often sends variables.

Bad:

```json
{
  "approvedBy": "manager-123",
  "tenantId": "tenant-b",
  "approvalLevel": 999,
  "status": "APPROVED"
}
```

Good:

```text
Client may submit:
  decision
  comment
  selected reason code

Server derives:
  approvedBy
  tenantId
  approvalLevel
  status
  timestamp
```

Validation pattern:

```java
public Map<String, Object> buildSafeCompletionVariables(
        SecurityActor actor,
        CompleteApprovalRequest request,
        CaseRecord caseRecord
) {
    return Map.of(
            "decision", request.decision(),
            "comment", sanitizeComment(request.comment()),
            "approvedBy", actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
            "tenantId", caseRecord.tenantId(),
            "approvedAt", Instant.now(clock).toString()
    );
}
```

**Invariant:**

```text
Never trust workflow security variables from client body.
```

---

# 23. Business Key Security

Business key is useful for correlation, but it is not authorization.

```text
businessKey = CASE-789
```

Risk:

```text
Attacker guesses businessKey and queries process/task.
```

Use:

```text
businessKey for correlation
tenantId + object policy for authorization
```

Safe lookup:

```java
ProcessInstanceRef instance =
        workflowGateway.findByBusinessKeyForTenant(businessKey, actorTenant)
                .orElseThrow(NotFoundException::new);
```

---

# 24. External Task / Job Worker Authorization

For Camunda 7 external tasks or Camunda 8 job workers, workers are service actors.

Threats:

```text
- worker fetches jobs for unauthorized tenant
- worker completes job for wrong business object
- worker uses system privilege to bypass domain rules
- worker retries unsafe command repeatedly
```

Policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canWorkerProcessJob(
        SecurityActor worker,
        WorkflowJobRef job
) {
    if (worker.effectiveActor().actorType() != ActorType.SERVICE) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("actor_not_service");
    }

    if (!worker.hasPermission("workflow.job.handle." + job.type())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_job_permission");
    }

    if (!worker.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(job.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("worker_tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("worker_job_allowed");
}
```

For internal state changes, use idempotency:

```text
jobKey + tenantId + businessResourceId + operation
```

---

# 25. Service Worker as System Actor

Some tasks are system tasks, not user tasks.

Audit must distinguish:

```text
SYSTEM action:
  workflow timeout handler closed stale case

USER action:
  Alice approved case

DELEGATED action:
  case-orchestrator completed task on behalf of Alice
```

Audit example:

```json
{
  "eventType": "WORKFLOW_JOB_COMPLETED",
  "actorType": "SERVICE",
  "serviceName": "case-worker-service",
  "tenantId": "tenant-a",
  "jobType": "case-timeout-handler",
  "processInstanceId": "2251799813685250",
  "businessKey": "CASE-789",
  "businessResourceType": "case",
  "businessResourceId": "CASE-789",
  "decision": "ALLOW",
  "reason": "worker_job_allowed"
}
```

---

# 26. Delegated Workflow Completion

Pattern:

```text
User Alice -> Orchestrator Service -> Workflow API -> complete task
```

Must check both:

```text
calling service is allowed to complete delegated task
AND
original user is allowed to perform business action
```

Policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canCompleteDelegatedTask(
        DelegatedSecurityActor actor,
        WorkflowTaskRef task,
        CaseRecord caseRecord
) {
    if (!actor.callingService().hasPermission("workflow.task.complete.delegated")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("calling_service_not_allowed");
    }

    SecurityActor originalUser = actor.originalUserActor();

    AuthorizationDecision taskDecision =
            taskPolicy.canCompleteTask(originalUser, task, BusinessObjectRef.caseRef(caseRecord));

    AuthorizationDecision caseDecision =
            caseApprovalPolicy.canApproveCase(originalUser, caseRecord);

    return AuthorizationDecision.allOf(taskDecision, caseDecision);
}
```

Audit must include:

```text
callingService
originalUser
effectiveActor
delegationId
taskId
businessResourceId
```

---

# 27. Task Search Authorization

Task list/search is high-risk because it can leak workload, customers, process names, counts, SLA.

Criteria:

```java
public record TaskSearchCriteria(
        String tenantId,
        String actorId,
        Set<String> actorGroups,
        boolean canReadAllTenantTasks,
        Set<String> processDefinitionKeys,
        int limit,
        int offset
) {}
```

Policy:

```text
If actor has workflow.task.read.all:
  allow tenant task list

Else:
  only tasks assigned to actor
  OR candidate user actor
  OR candidate group intersects actor groups
```

For Camunda 7 embedded:

```java
TaskQuery query = taskService.createTaskQuery()
        .taskTenantIdIn(criteria.tenantId())
        .active();

if (!criteria.canReadAllTenantTasks()) {
    query.or()
        .taskAssignee(criteria.actorId())
        .taskCandidateUser(criteria.actorId())
    .endOr();
}
```

Also filter by business object visibility if tasks correspond to domain data.

---

# 28. Task Count Leakage

Bad:

```text
GET /tasks/count returns all open tasks in tenant.
```

Safer:

```text
count only visible tasks under same criteria as list.
```

Rule:

```text
task list and task count must use same visibility policy.
```

Audit suspicious count probing:

```text
many queries with different filters
cross-tenant tenantId attempts
large pagination attempts
```

---

# 29. Process Instance Query Authorization

Process instance query should bind:

```text
tenant
process definition
business key
business object visibility
actor permission
```

Policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canReadProcessInstance(
        SecurityActor actor,
        WorkflowProcessInstanceRef instance,
        BusinessObjectRef businessObject
) {
    if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.process.read")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_process_read");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(instance.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("process_tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!businessObject.tenantId().equals(instance.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("process_business_tenant_mismatch");
    }

    if (!businessObjectAccessPolicy.canRead(actor, businessObject).allowed()) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("business_object_not_visible");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("process_instance_read_allowed");
}
```

---

# 30. Process Definition Start Policy

Not every user who can see a process definition may start it.

```text
READ_PROCESS_DEFINITION != START_PROCESS
```

Suggested permission:

```text
workflow.process.start.<processDefinitionKey>
```

or catalog row:

| Process | Permission | Tenant Scope |
|---|---|---|
| `caseApproval` | `workflow.process.start.case-approval` | tenant |
| `orderFulfillment` | `workflow.process.start.order-fulfillment` | tenant |
| `userOffboarding` | `workflow.process.start.user-offboarding` | admin |

---

# 31. Camunda 8 Tasklist Permission Mapping

Camunda 8.9 Tasklist docs list common user task operations and their required permissions, such as `READ`, `CLAIM`, `COMPLETE`, and `UPDATE` on `USER_TASK`, plus corresponding process-definition permissions like `READ_USER_TASK`, `CLAIM_USER_TASK`, `COMPLETE_USER_TASK`, and `UPDATE_USER_TASK`. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/tasklist/user-task-authorization/))

In your Jersey API, map these concepts to your own internal permission catalog:

| Camunda/Tasklist Concept | App Permission |
|---|---|
| read task | `workflow.task.read` |
| claim task | `workflow.task.claim` |
| complete task | `workflow.task.complete` |
| update/assign task | `workflow.task.assign` / `workflow.task.reassign` |
| read process tasks | `workflow.process.read-tasks` |
| complete process task broadly | avoid broad permission unless admin/supervisor |

---

# 32. Camunda 8 Tenant Checks

Camunda 8 docs state multi-tenancy checks enforce tenant-based access control, but are disabled by default; when enabled, the system verifies users can only access resources associated with assigned tenants. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/multi-tenancy/))

Therefore:

```text
Application-level tenant check remains mandatory.
Camunda-level tenant check is defense-in-depth / platform-level access control.
```

In custom Jersey API:

```java
if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(task.tenantId())) {
    throw new AccessDeniedException("task_tenant_not_allowed");
}
```

---

# 33. Assignment Source Security

Assignment can come from:

```text
static BPMN assignee/candidateGroups
process variables
task listener
external directory lookup
business rule
manual reassignment
```

Camunda 7 docs state assignment expressions can use process variables or call beans/services, and task listeners can set assignee/candidate users/groups. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/reference/bpmn20/tasks/user-task/))

Threats:

```text
- process variable manipulates assignee
- user-controlled payload sets candidate group
- task listener trusts unsafe variable
- external directory returns broad group
```

Controls:

```text
- do not let client set assignee/candidate directly unless authorized
- validate assignment target belongs to tenant
- validate candidate group is allowed for process/task
- audit assignment changes
- use server-derived variables for assignment
```

---

# 34. Assignment Change Audit

```json
{
  "eventType": "WORKFLOW_TASK_REASSIGNED",
  "tenantId": "tenant-a",
  "taskId": "task-123",
  "processInstanceId": "pi-456",
  "businessKey": "CASE-789",
  "oldAssignee": "alice",
  "newAssignee": "bob",
  "changedBy": "supervisor-1",
  "reason": "out_of_office",
  "decision": "ALLOW",
  "policyName": "workflow-task-assignment-policy",
  "policyVersion": "2026.07.1"
}
```

---

# 35. Workflow Error Semantics

| Case | Status | Reason |
|---|---:|---|
| Task not found in actor tenant | 404 | `task_not_found` |
| Task belongs to another tenant | 404 or 403 | `task_tenant_not_allowed` |
| Task already completed | 409 | `task_not_active` |
| User not assignee/candidate | 403 | `not_task_assignee_or_candidate` |
| Missing workflow permission | 403 | `missing_workflow_task_complete` |
| Missing domain permission | 403 | `missing_case_approve` |
| Business object invalid state | 409 | `case_not_submitted` |
| Maker-checker violation | 403 | `maker_checker_violation` |
| Camunda command conflict | 409 | `workflow_command_conflict` |
| Camunda unavailable | 503 | `workflow_engine_unavailable` |

---

# 36. Idempotency for Complete/Start Commands

Workflow commands can be retried by clients.

Use idempotency for:

```text
POST /tasks/{taskId}/complete
POST /processes/{key}/start
POST /process-instances/{id}/cancel
POST /tasks/{taskId}/assign
```

Key:

```text
clientId/userId + operation + taskId/processInstanceId + requestHash
```

Example:

```java
String idempotencyScope = "%s:%s:%s".formatted(
        actor.effectiveActor().subject(),
        "workflow.task.complete",
        taskId
);
```

If task already completed:

```text
same idempotency key + same payload:
  return previous result

different payload:
  422

no idempotency but task already completed:
  409
```

---

# 37. Outbox/Saga for Camunda + Domain DB

If Camunda command succeeds but DB update fails, or DB succeeds but Camunda fails, state may diverge.

Pattern A - Domain first + outbox:

```text
1. Validate authz.
2. Write domain intent + outbox event in DB.
3. Worker completes Camunda task.
4. Worker marks intent completed.
```

Pattern B - Camunda first + reconciliation:

```text
1. Validate authz.
2. Complete Camunda task.
3. Update domain DB.
4. If DB fails, reconciliation job repairs/flags incident.
```

Recommended for high-risk actions:

```text
Use idempotency + outbox + reconciliation.
Audit both authorization decision and command result.
```

---

# 38. Incident / Retry Authorization

Retrying failed jobs or resolving incidents is privileged.

Permissions:

```text
workflow.incident.read
workflow.incident.resolve
workflow.job.retry
workflow.job.cancel
```

Policy:

```java
public AuthorizationDecision canRetryWorkflowJob(
        SecurityActor actor,
        WorkflowIncidentRef incident
) {
    if (!actor.hasPermission("workflow.job.retry")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_workflow_job_retry");
    }

    if (!actor.tenantContext().canAccessTenant(incident.tenantId())) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("incident_tenant_not_allowed");
    }

    if (!actor.hasPermission("support.workflow.operate")) {
        return AuthorizationDecision.deny("missing_support_workflow_operate");
    }

    return AuthorizationDecision.allow("workflow_job_retry_allowed");
}
```

Retry action must be audited because it can re-execute side effects.

---

# 39. Testing Matrix

```text
Task read/search
[ ] User sees assigned task.
[ ] User sees candidate group task.
[ ] User does not see unrelated same-tenant task.
[ ] User does not see cross-tenant task.
[ ] Count matches visible task list.

Claim
[ ] Candidate user can claim.
[ ] Candidate group member can claim.
[ ] Non-candidate cannot claim.
[ ] Already assigned task cannot be claimed by other user.
[ ] Cross-tenant task claim denied.

Complete
[ ] Assignee can complete if domain policy allows.
[ ] Candidate can complete only if allowed by policy.
[ ] Missing workflow.task.complete denied.
[ ] Missing domain permission denied.
[ ] Maker-checker violation denied.
[ ] Invalid business state returns 409.
[ ] Completed task returns 409/idempotent response.

Assign/reassign
[ ] Supervisor can reassign within tenant.
[ ] Non-supervisor denied.
[ ] New assignee outside tenant denied.
[ ] Reassignment audited.

Process
[ ] User can start allowed process in own tenant.
[ ] User cannot start process in other tenant.
[ ] User cannot cancel process without permission.
[ ] Business object visibility required for process instance read.

Service worker
[ ] Service actor can handle allowed job type.
[ ] User token rejected for worker endpoint.
[ ] Service outside tenant scope denied.

Delegation
[ ] Calling service allowed but original user denied => deny.
[ ] Original user allowed but calling service denied => deny.
[ ] Actor chain audited.
```

---

# 40. Example Test - Non-Candidate Cannot Complete

```java
@Test
void nonCandidateShouldNotCompleteTask() {
    SecurityActor alice = PrincipalFixture.user()
            .subject("alice")
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .permission("workflow.task.complete")
            .permission("case.approve")
            .groups(Set.of("sales"))
            .build();

    WorkflowTaskRef task = TaskFixture.active()
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .assignee(null)
            .candidateGroups(Set.of("risk-reviewers"))
            .businessResourceId("CASE-1")
            .build();

    CaseRecord caseRecord = CaseFixture.submitted()
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .id("CASE-1")
            .createdBy("bob")
            .build();

    AuthorizationDecision decision =
            taskPolicy.canCompleteTask(alice, task, BusinessObjectRef.caseRef(caseRecord));

    assertFalse(decision.allowed());
    assertEquals("not_allowed_to_complete_task", decision.reason());
}
```

---

# 41. Example Test - Maker-Checker Denied

```java
@Test
void creatorShouldNotApproveOwnCaseEvenIfTaskAssigned() {
    SecurityActor alice = PrincipalFixture.user()
            .subject("alice")
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .permission("workflow.task.complete")
            .permission("case.approve")
            .build();

    WorkflowTaskRef task = TaskFixture.active()
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .assignee("alice")
            .businessResourceId("CASE-1")
            .build();

    CaseRecord caseRecord = CaseFixture.submitted()
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .id("CASE-1")
            .createdBy("alice")
            .build();

    AuthorizationDecision taskDecision =
            taskPolicy.canCompleteTask(alice, task, BusinessObjectRef.caseRef(caseRecord));

    AuthorizationDecision caseDecision =
            caseApprovalPolicy.canApproveCase(alice, caseRecord);

    AuthorizationDecision finalDecision =
            AuthorizationDecision.allOf(taskDecision, caseDecision);

    assertFalse(finalDecision.allowed());
    assertEquals("maker_checker_violation", finalDecision.reason());
}
```

---

# 42. Example Test - Cross-Tenant Task Concealment

```java
@Test
void crossTenantTaskShouldReturn404AndAuditDeny() {
    SecurityActor alice = PrincipalFixture.user()
            .subject("alice")
            .tenantId("tenant-a")
            .permission("workflow.task.read")
            .build();

    String tenantBTaskId = seed.task()
            .tenantId("tenant-b")
            .assignee("charlie")
            .build()
            .taskId();

    Response response = target("/tasks/" + tenantBTaskId)
            .request()
            .header(HttpHeaders.AUTHORIZATION, tokenFor(alice))
            .get();

    assertEquals(404, response.getStatus());

    SecurityAuditEvent event = auditSink.lastEvent();
    assertEquals("WORKFLOW_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED", event.eventType());
    assertEquals("task_tenant_not_allowed", event.reason());
}
```

---

# 43. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Risiko | Perbaikan |
|---|---|---|
| `complete(taskId)` langsung dari controller | task hijacking | load task + policy |
| Candidate group dianggap final authz | domain bypass | task policy + business policy |
| Tasklist UI dianggap security untuk custom API | API bypass | enforce in Jersey service |
| User-controlled variables set `tenantId`/`approvedBy` | spoofing | server-derived variables |
| Complete task tanpa business object load | BOLA/domain bypass | load business object by tenant |
| Process instance read by ID only | cross-tenant leak | tenant + business visibility |
| Reassign task tanpa supervisor policy | privilege escalation | reassign policy |
| Service worker all tenants | lateral blast radius | service tenant scope |
| Retry incident tanpa audit | side-effect replay | incident permission + audit |
| List tasks but count all tasks | workload leakage | same visibility criteria |

---

# 44. Code Review Checklist

```text
[ ] Endpoint has workflow permission annotation.
[ ] Task/process ID from client is treated as hostile.
[ ] Task is loaded with tenant/process/business metadata.
[ ] Actor tenant matches task tenant.
[ ] Business object is loaded by tenant.
[ ] Task policy checks assignee/candidate/group.
[ ] Business policy checks domain permission/state.
[ ] Maker-checker/four-eyes rule applied where needed.
[ ] Client cannot set security-sensitive variables.
[ ] Assignment/reassignment validates target user/group.
[ ] Service worker has ActorType.SERVICE and tenant scope.
[ ] Delegated call checks service and original user.
[ ] Task list/count use same visibility criteria.
[ ] Complete/start/cancel commands are idempotent where retryable.
[ ] Authorization decision and workflow command result are audited.
```

---

# 45. Minimal Production Invariants

```text
1. Task ID alone is never enough to complete/read/assign a task.
2. Task tenant, process tenant, and business object tenant must match.
3. User must have workflow permission and domain permission.
4. Assignee/candidate/group relation must be checked for user tasks.
5. Candidate group metadata is not a substitute for business authorization.
6. Client-submitted variables must not control tenant, actor, approval level, or status.
7. Reassignment/escalation requires separate permission and audit.
8. Service workers must have service identity, job permission, and tenant scope.
9. Delegated workflow actions must authorize both service and original user.
10. Every workflow authorization decision must be auditable by taskId, processInstanceId, businessKey, actor, tenant, and reason.
```

---

# 46. Roadmap Singkat

| Urutan | Fokus | Target |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | User task mental model | assignee/candidate/group tidak disalahartikan |
| 2 | Workflow permission catalog | task/process/job permissions jelas |
| 3 | Task gateway abstraction | Camunda 7/8 detail tidak bocor ke domain |
| 4 | Task policy | claim/read/complete/assign rules |
| 5 | Business policy | case/order/domain rules |
| 6 | Tenant/process/business binding | task tenant = object tenant |
| 7 | Variables security | client tidak spoof actor/tenant/status |
| 8 | Service worker authz | job type + tenant scope |
| 9 | Idempotency/outbox | retry aman |
| 10 | Audit/tests | workflow abuse cases tertutup |

# Cheatsheet - Authorization Observability & Incident Response di Jersey/JAX-RS Microservices

## 1. Tujuan

Authorization observability membantu kita menjawab:

```text id="pdq1nq"
Apakah ada token attack?
Apakah ada cross-tenant probing?
Apakah ada BOLA/IDOR attempt?
Apakah permission revoke sudah efektif?
Apakah PDP/entitlement service sedang bermasalah?
Apakah service internal disalahgunakan?
Apakah audit cukup untuk investigasi?
Apa yang harus dilakukan saat alert terjadi?
```

NIST SP 800-61 Rev. 3 memosisikan incident response sebagai bagian dari cybersecurity risk management yang membantu organisasi mempersiapkan response, mengurangi dampak incident, serta meningkatkan deteksi, response, dan recovery. Untuk application security logging, OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet memberi panduan event apa yang perlu dicatat dan bagaimana log digunakan untuk detection/response. ([NIST Computer Security Resource Center](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/61/r3/final?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Mental Model

```text id="ocd2lf"
Logs:
  What happened?

Metrics:
  How often / how bad / is it spiking?

Traces:
  Where did request flow?

Audit:
  Who did what to which resource, and why was it allowed/denied?

Alerts:
  Which signal requires action?

Runbook:
  What should on-call/security do next?

Incident response:
  Triage -> contain -> eradicate -> recover -> learn.
```

OpenTelemetry menyediakan framework vendor-neutral untuk mengumpulkan telemetry seperti traces, metrics, dan logs; semantic conventions membantu standardisasi nama atribut agar konsisten di codebase/platform. ([OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 3. Big Picture

```mermaid id="n7p0ht"
flowchart TD
    A[Request] --> B[Jersey AuthN Filter]
    B --> C[Token/API Key/HMAC/mTLS Validation]
    C --> D[SecurityActor]

    D --> E[Jersey AuthZ Filter]
    E --> F[Application Policy]
    F --> G[Object/Tenant/Workflow Policy]
    G --> H[MyBatis/PostgreSQL]

    B --> I[Security Logs]
    E --> I
    F --> I
    G --> I

    B --> J[Metrics]
    E --> J
    F --> J
    G --> J

    A --> K[Trace/Span]
    K --> B
    K --> E
    K --> F
    K --> H

    I --> L[SIEM / Log Analytics]
    J --> M[Metrics Backend]
    K --> N[Tracing Backend]

    L --> O[Alerts]
    M --> O
    N --> O

    O --> P[Runbook]
    P --> Q[Incident Response]
```

---

# 4. Observability Signals untuk AuthN/AuthZ

| Signal | Tujuan |
|---|---|
| Authentication failures | deteksi token abuse, expired token spike, config issue |
| Authorization denies | deteksi missing permission, policy change, probing |
| Cross-tenant denies | deteksi tenant probing/BOLA |
| Object-level denies | deteksi IDOR attempt |
| PDP/entitlement failures | deteksi dependency risk/fail-closed storm |
| Cache stale/version mismatch | deteksi revocation working atau cache issue |
| Internal endpoint misuse | user token mencoba service endpoint |
| Partner/HMAC failures | invalid signature/replay/tampering |
| Admin/permission changes | forensic governance |
| Impersonation/delegation events | support abuse/confused deputy |
| Workflow denies | unauthorized complete/reassign/escalate |

OWASP API Security 2023 menempatkan BOLA sebagai API1 dan Broken Authentication sebagai API2; keduanya sangat cocok dipantau lewat signal seperti object ID probing, cross-tenant deny, invalid token, wrong audience, dan token replay. ([OWASP Foundation](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 5. Logging vs Audit vs Metrics

| Output | Contoh | Retention | Audience |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Application log | `invalid_token` | short/medium | developer/SRE |
| Security audit | `AUTHORIZATION_DECISION DENY` | medium/long | security/compliance |
| Metric | `authz_denied_total{reason}` | medium | SRE/security |
| Trace span | `authz.policy.evaluate` | short/medium | SRE/dev |
| Alert | `cross_tenant_deny_spike` | incident lifecycle | on-call/security |
| Report | weekly authz trends | long | engineering leadership |

Rule:

```text id="ehtdua"
Audit answers "who/what/why".
Metrics answer "how much/how fast".
Logs answer "what happened around this event".
Traces answer "where did it happen in the call chain".
```

---

# 6. Security Event Vocabulary

OWASP Application Logging Vocabulary Cheat Sheet proposes a standard vocabulary for security events to simplify monitoring and alerting. Use consistent event names instead of random log strings. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Vocabulary_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Recommended event names:

```text id="6x8wfz"
AUTHENTICATION_SUCCESS
AUTHENTICATION_FAILURE
TOKEN_VALIDATION_FAILURE
AUTHORIZATION_DECISION
AUTHORIZATION_DENIED
CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED
OBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED
PDP_FAILURE
ENTITLEMENT_CACHE_STALE
PERMISSION_REVOKED
ROLE_ASSIGNED
ACL_GRANT_CREATED
ACL_GRANT_REVOKED
INTERNAL_ENDPOINT_DENIED
SERVICE_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED
PARTNER_SIGNATURE_INVALID
REPLAY_DETECTED
IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT
IMPERSONATION_STARTED
IMPERSONATION_DENIED
DELEGATED_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED
WORKFLOW_TASK_COMPLETED
WORKFLOW_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED
BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS
```

# 7. Structured Security Log Schema

```java id="8d6chy"
public record SecurityEvent(
        String eventType,
        String eventId,
        Instant eventTime,
        String severity,
        String correlationId,
        String traceId,
        String spanId,

        ActorInfo actor,
        RequestInfo request,
        ResourceInfo resource,

        String action,
        String decision,
        String reason,

        String policyName,
        String policyVersion,
        String decisionSource,

        Map<String, Object> evidence
) {}
```

Actor:

```java id="6gmgyw"
public record ActorInfo(
        String actorType,
        String subjectId,
        String username,
        String tenantId,
        String clientId,
        String serviceName,
        String partnerId,
        String originalActorId,
        String effectiveActorId,
        String callingService
) {}
```

Request:

```java id="owc4pt"
public record RequestInfo(
        String method,
        String pathTemplate,
        String sourceIp,
        String userAgent,
        String origin,
        String requestId,
        String authScheme
) {}
```

Resource:

```java id="tyf2c4"
public record ResourceInfo(
        String tenantId,
        String resourceType,
        String resourceId,
        String processInstanceId,
        String taskId,
        String businessKey
) {}
```

# 8. Jangan Log Data Sensitif

Jangan log:

```text id="9v622h"
- raw JWT
- refresh token
- API key
- HMAC secret
- Authorization header
- cookie value
- password/OTP
- private key
- full request body
- sensitive claim dump
- SQL with bound secret parameter
```

Boleh log metadata aman:

```text id="sxpmpl"
- issuer
- audience validity result
- kid
- token jti hash
- clientId
- tenantId
- subjectId
- reason code
- policy version
- correlationId
```

OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet menekankan logging perlu membantu security monitoring tetapi juga harus menghindari data sensitif yang tidak seharusnya dicatat. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

# 9. Metrics Naming

Gunakan nama stabil dan label terbatas.

```text id="545dqv"
authn_success_total
authn_failure_total{reason, issuer, client_type}

token_validation_failure_total{reason, issuer}
jwt_unknown_kid_total{issuer}
jwt_invalid_signature_total{issuer}
jwt_wrong_audience_total{issuer, audience_group}

authz_decision_total{decision, action, resource_type}
authz_denied_total{reason, action, resource_type}
authz_cross_tenant_denied_total{action, resource_type}
authz_object_denied_total{action, resource_type}

pdp_request_total{source}
pdp_failure_total{reason, source}
pdp_latency_ms{source}

entitlement_cache_hit_total{cache}
entitlement_cache_miss_total{cache}
entitlement_version_mismatch_total

internal_endpoint_denied_total{reason}
partner_signature_invalid_total{client_id}
replay_detected_total{client_id, operation}

workflow_authz_denied_total{reason, task_definition_key}
```

Jangan pakai high-cardinality label:

```text id="d9xltw"
Bad labels:
  userId
  caseId
  raw path /cases/CASE-123
  token jti
  full IP
```

Gunakan:

```text id="0n2doo"
path_template = /cases/{caseId}
resource_type = case
reason = missing_permission
```

# 10. Trace Spans untuk AuthN/AuthZ

Recommended spans:

```text id="s4fh83"
http.request
  authn.validate_token
  authn.resolve_principal
  authz.endpoint_check
  authz.object_policy
  authz.entitlement_lookup
  authz.acl_lookup
  db.mybatis.query
  audit.emit
```

Attributes:

```text id="qfd4h7"
security.auth.scheme = bearer
security.authn.result = success/failure
security.authz.decision = allow/deny
security.authz.reason = missing_permission
security.policy.name = case-access-policy
security.policy.version = 2026.07.1
enduser.id = hashed/stable safe id if allowed
tenant.id = tenant-a
```

OpenTelemetry semantic conventions provide common attribute names for operations and telemetry; for custom security attributes, keep naming consistent and avoid raw secrets/high-cardinality values. ([OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/semantic-conventions/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

# 11. Instrumentation di AuthenticationFilter

```java id="0pl7oz"
@Provider
@Priority(Priorities.AUTHENTICATION)
public class AuthenticationFilter implements ContainerRequestFilter {

    private final MeterRegistry metrics;
    private final SecurityAuditLogger auditLogger;
    private final AccessTokenValidator tokenValidator;
    private final PrincipalMapper principalMapper;

    @Override
    public void filter(ContainerRequestContext ctx) {
        String correlationId = CorrelationIds.current();

        try {
            String token = extractBearer(ctx);
            VerifiedAccessToken verified = tokenValidator.validate(token);
            SecurityActor actor = principalMapper.map(verified, ctx);

            ctx.setSecurityContext(new RequestSecurityContext(actor, true, "Bearer"));

            metrics.counter("authn_success_total",
                    "auth_scheme", "bearer",
                    "issuer", safeIssuer(verified.issuer())
            ).increment();

            auditLogger.logAuthenticationSuccess(actor, correlationId);

        } catch (InvalidAccessTokenException e) {
            metrics.counter("authn_failure_total",
                    "reason", e.reason(),
                    "auth_scheme", "bearer"
            ).increment();

            auditLogger.logAuthenticationFailure(
                    e.reason(),
                    requestSummary(ctx),
                    correlationId
            );

            abortUnauthorized(ctx, correlationId);
        }
    }
}
```

---

# 12. Instrumentation di AuthorizationFilter

```java id="a0sc6p"
@Provider
@Priority(Priorities.AUTHORIZATION)
public class AuthorizationFilter implements ContainerRequestFilter {

    private final AuthorizationService authorizationService;
    private final SecurityAuditLogger auditLogger;
    private final MeterRegistry metrics;

    @Override
    public void filter(ContainerRequestContext ctx) {
        SecurityActor actor = SecurityPrincipals.requireActor(ctx.getSecurityContext());
        RequiredPermission required = RequiredPermissionResolver.resolve(ctx);

        AuthorizationDecision decision =
                authorizationService.checkEndpointPermission(actor, required.permission());

        metrics.counter("authz_decision_total",
                "decision", decision.allowed() ? "allow" : "deny",
                "action", required.permission(),
                "resource_type", required.resourceTypeOrUnknown()
        ).increment();

        if (!decision.allowed()) {
            metrics.counter("authz_denied_total",
                    "reason", decision.reason(),
                    "action", required.permission(),
                    "resource_type", required.resourceTypeOrUnknown()
            ).increment();
        }

        auditLogger.logAuthorizationDecision(
                actor,
                required.permission(),
                ResourceRef.endpoint(ctx.getUriInfo().getPath()),
                decision,
                CorrelationIds.current()
        );

        if (!decision.allowed()) {
            throw new AccessDeniedException(decision.reason());
        }
    }
}
```

---

# 13. Object/Tenant Deny Instrumentation

```java id="1510ew"
public void auditAndDenyObjectAccess(
        SecurityActor actor,
        String action,
        ResourceRef resource,
        AuthorizationDecision decision
) {
    auditLogger.logAuthorizationDecision(actor, action, resource, decision);

    metrics.counter("authz_object_denied_total",
            "reason", decision.reason(),
            "action", action,
            "resource_type", resource.resourceType()
    ).increment();

    if ("tenant_not_allowed".equals(decision.reason())
            || "cross_tenant_access_denied".equals(decision.reason())) {
        metrics.counter("authz_cross_tenant_denied_total",
                "action", action,
                "resource_type", resource.resourceType()
        ).increment();
    }

    throw new AccessDeniedException(decision.reason());
}
```

# 14. Alert Catalog

| Alert | Signal | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| Invalid token spike | `authn_failure_total{reason}` above baseline | Medium/High |
| Wrong audience spike | `jwt_wrong_audience_total` | High |
| Unknown kid spike | `jwt_unknown_kid_total` | High |
| Cross-tenant deny spike | `authz_cross_tenant_denied_total` | High/Critical |
| Object deny spike | `authz_object_denied_total` | Medium/High |
| PDP timeout spike | `pdp_failure_total{reason=timeout}` | High |
| Fail-closed storm | `authz_denied_total{reason=pdp_timeout_fail_closed}` | High |
| Admin permission changes | `ROLE_ASSIGNED`, `PERMISSION_GRANTED` | Medium/High |
| Break-glass access | `BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS` | Critical |
| Internal endpoint misuse | `internal_endpoint_denied_total` | High |
| Replay detected | `replay_detected_total` | High |
| Invalid partner signature spike | `partner_signature_invalid_total` | High |
| Audit sink failure | `audit_emit_failure_total` | Critical |

---

# 15. Alert Threshold Examples

```text id="5gwd40"
Invalid token spike:
  authn_failure_total{reason="invalid_signature"} > 5x 1h baseline for 10m

Unknown kid spike:
  jwt_unknown_kid_total > 20 in 5m

Cross-tenant deny:
  authz_cross_tenant_denied_total > 10 in 5m
  OR any single actor has > 5 distinct resource IDs denied in 5m

PDP failure:
  pdp_failure_total{reason="timeout"} > 5% of PDP requests for 5m

Break-glass:
  any BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS event

Permission change:
  admin.role.assign outside business hours
```

Threshold should be baseline-driven. Start conservative, then tune based on false positives.

---

# 16. Detection Rules - Cross-Tenant Probing

Pattern:

```text id="nm4ajs"
same actor
many resource IDs
same endpoint/resource type
tenant mismatch or 404-concealed denies
short time window
```

Pseudo query:

```sql id="t0t9c1"
SELECT
    actor_subject_id,
    actor_tenant_id,
    action,
    resource_type,
    count(*) AS deny_count,
    count(DISTINCT resource_id) AS distinct_resources,
    min(event_time) AS first_seen,
    max(event_time) AS last_seen
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type = 'CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED'
  AND event_time >= now() - interval '10 minutes'
GROUP BY actor_subject_id, actor_tenant_id, action, resource_type
HAVING count(*) >= 5
   AND count(DISTINCT resource_id) >= 5;
```

Response:

```text id="k3l1fv"
- check actor/session/client
- inspect source IP/user agent
- revoke token/session if malicious
- verify endpoint object-level policy works
- check whether object IDs are enumerable
```

---

# 17. Detection Rules - Token Attack

Signals:

```text id="cdii0i"
invalid_signature
wrong_audience
wrong_issuer
expired_token spike
unknown_kid
alg_disallowed
token_type_invalid
permission_version_stale
```

Pseudo query:

```sql id="cv5t7z"
SELECT
    reason,
    issuer,
    client_id,
    source_ip_hash,
    count(*) AS failures
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type = 'TOKEN_VALIDATION_FAILURE'
  AND event_time >= now() - interval '15 minutes'
GROUP BY reason, issuer, client_id, source_ip_hash
HAVING count(*) > 20;
```

Interpretation:

| Reason | Likely Cause |
|---|---|
| `expired_token` | normal expiry, client refresh issue |
| `wrong_audience` | token substitution / config mismatch |
| `invalid_signature` | forged/stale key/config issue |
| `unknown_key_id` | IdP rotation issue or attack |
| `disallowed_alg` | malicious token |
| `permission_version_stale` | revocation working |

---

# 18. Detection Rules - Confused Deputy

Pattern:

```text id="xm8c7o"
delegated call
calling service allowed
original user denied
repeated attempts
```

Query:

```sql id="1cmxzg"
SELECT
    calling_service,
    original_actor_id,
    action,
    resource_type,
    count(*) AS deny_count
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type = 'DELEGATED_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED'
  AND reason IN ('original_actor_not_authorized', 'missing_original_actor')
  AND event_time >= now() - interval '30 minutes'
GROUP BY calling_service, original_actor_id, action, resource_type
HAVING count(*) >= 3;
```

Response:

```text id="gkixyd"
- inspect upstream service request path
- verify original actor propagation
- check if orchestrator endpoint lacks user-side guard
- disable/degrade delegated operation if abuse active
```

---

# 19. Detection Rules - Internal Endpoint Misuse

Pattern:

```text id="yqjdnk"
user token used on /internal/*
external source IP hits internal route
missing mTLS where required
service not allowlisted
```

Query:

```sql id="zfc74d"
SELECT
    actor_type,
    path_template,
    reason,
    count(*) AS count
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type = 'INTERNAL_ENDPOINT_DENIED'
  AND event_time >= now() - interval '15 minutes'
GROUP BY actor_type, path_template, reason
HAVING count(*) >= 3;
```

Response:

```text id="ixdujr"
- verify gateway blocks /internal/*
- verify ingress route not exposed
- verify spoofable headers stripped
- revoke suspicious token/session
- inspect source service/pod/IP
```

---

# 20. Detection Rules - Permission Change Abuse

High-risk events:

```text id="fr0sej"
ROLE_ASSIGNED
ROLE_REVOKED
PERMISSION_GRANTED
PERMISSION_REVOKED
ACL_GRANT_CREATED
ACL_GRANT_REVOKED
TENANT_ADMIN_ASSIGNED
BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS
IMPERSONATION_STARTED
```

Query:

```sql id="l38c83"
SELECT
    event_type,
    changed_by,
    target_subject_id,
    tenant_id,
    count(*) AS changes
FROM entitlement_audit_event
WHERE event_time >= now() - interval '1 hour'
GROUP BY event_type, changed_by, target_subject_id, tenant_id
HAVING count(*) >= 10;
```

Additional alert:

```text id="9s88ku"
permission grant outside business hours
admin assigns role to self
new admin immediately performs sensitive export
break-glass without ticket
```

---

# 21. Detection Rules - Partner/Webhook Abuse

Signals:

```text id="f1unkd"
PARTNER_SIGNATURE_INVALID
REPLAY_DETECTED
IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT
partner_tenant_denied
payload_too_large
```

Query:

```sql id="r57w3e"
SELECT
    client_id,
    event_type,
    reason,
    count(*) AS events
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type IN (
    'PARTNER_SIGNATURE_INVALID',
    'REPLAY_DETECTED',
    'IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT',
    'PARTNER_TENANT_DENIED'
)
AND event_time >= now() - interval '15 minutes'
GROUP BY client_id, event_type, reason
HAVING count(*) >= 5;
```

Response:

```text id="p3thp9"
- check partner integration deployment/change
- rotate partner secret if capture suspected
- block client_id temporarily if active abuse
- verify idempotency prevents double mutation
- contact partner owner
```

---

# 22. Detection Rules - Workflow Abuse

Signals:

```text id="ejoll9"
workflow task complete denied
task tenant mismatch
not assignee/candidate
maker-checker violation
process cancel denied
incident retry spike
```

Query:

```sql id="u59lg4"
SELECT
    actor_subject_id,
    action,
    task_definition_key,
    reason,
    count(*) AS deny_count
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type = 'WORKFLOW_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED'
  AND event_time >= now() - interval '30 minutes'
GROUP BY actor_subject_id, action, task_definition_key, reason
HAVING count(*) >= 5;
```

Response:

```text id="6gspaf"
- validate UI/tasklist state
- verify assignment/candidate group mapping
- inspect possible taskId enumeration
- check if user trying to bypass maker-checker
- confirm workflow task policy still matches business policy
```

# 23. Incident Severity Matrix

| Severity | Example |
|---|---|
| SEV-1 Critical | confirmed cross-tenant data access, forged token accepted, signing key leak, public internal endpoint exploited |
| SEV-2 High | repeated cross-tenant probing blocked, PDP outage causing deny storm, admin role abuse attempt |
| SEV-3 Medium | invalid token spike, partner signature errors, permission cache mismatch |
| SEV-4 Low | isolated denied access, expected expired token noise |

Decision factors:

```text id="qmlsxj"
data exposure?
privilege escalation?
tenant boundary crossed?
active exploitation?
control worked or failed?
blast radius?
credential/key compromise?
```

---

# 24. Incident Response Lifecycle for AuthN/AuthZ

Adapted to authN/authZ:

```text id="8a0qjq"
Preparation:
  logging, metrics, alerts, runbooks, access to audit data

Detection & Analysis:
  validate alert, scope actor/resource/tenant/time window

Containment:
  revoke token/session/API key, disable client/service, block route/IP, freeze permission changes

Eradication:
  patch policy/query/config, rotate key/secret, remove malicious grants

Recovery:
  redeploy, re-enable access carefully, monitor

Post-Incident:
  RCA, add test, update threat model/runbook, notify stakeholders
```

NIST SP 800-61 Rev. 3 is the current NIST incident response guidance and aligns incident response considerations with CSF 2.0 functions; use it as the governance-level reference while keeping application-specific runbooks concrete. ([NIST Computer Security Resource Center](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/61/r3/final?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 25. Runbook - Cross-Tenant Deny Spike

## Trigger

```text id="3qddc9"
authz_cross_tenant_denied_total spike
or CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED alert
```

## Triage

```text id="ro5tzd"
1. Identify actorId/clientId/serviceName.
2. Identify tenantId and targetTenantIds.
3. Count distinct resource IDs.
4. Check endpoints involved.
5. Check response returned 404/403 and no data returned.
6. Check recent release/config changes.
7. Check source IP/user agent/origin.
```

## Containment

```text id="sjwl8b"
- revoke actor session/token if malicious
- temporarily block client/IP if automated probing
- disable partner/client if external
- increase rate limit strictness
```

## Eradication

```text id="wj68ct"
- patch endpoint if policy gap found
- add object-level test
- add mapper tenant predicate test
- review cache keys
```

## Recovery

```text id="xxgcmu"
- redeploy fix
- monitor deny rate
- verify no data exposure
- document impact
```

---

# 26. Runbook - Unknown `kid` / Invalid Signature Spike

## Possible Causes

```text id="f98tl5"
- IdP key rotation misconfigured
- resource server JWKS cache stale
- attacker sending forged tokens
- wrong environment issuer/JWKS configured
```

## Triage

```text id="jwf764"
1. Check issuer affected.
2. Check kid values.
3. Check JWKS endpoint availability.
4. Check IdP rotation event/change.
5. Check whether valid users are failing.
6. Check if unknown kid is random/high entropy from attackers.
```

## Containment

```text id="g6q25y"
- if attack: rate-limit/block abusive sources
- if IdP rotation issue: publish correct JWKS / roll back signing key
- if app config issue: hotfix trusted JWKS URI/config
```

## Never

```text id="4g5hlt"
- disable signature validation
- allow unknown kid
- fetch JWKS from token-provided URL
- accept alg=none
```

---

# 27. Runbook - PDP/Entitlement Failure

## Trigger

```text id="knl7yu"
pdp_failure_total spike
authz_denied_total{reason=pdp_timeout_fail_closed} spike
```

## Triage

```text id="1wb9k4"
1. Check PDP/entitlement service health.
2. Check latency and timeout rates.
3. Check cache hit/miss.
4. Identify affected endpoints/actions.
5. Determine if sensitive actions are failing closed.
6. Confirm no fail-open path exists.
```

## Containment

```text id="l1a9gd"
- scale/restart PDP if dependency issue
- enable read-only degraded mode only if policy allows
- block high-risk writes if PDP unavailable
- communicate expected 403/503 increase
```

## Recovery

```text id="kx7989"
- restore PDP
- clear bad cache if needed
- replay failed non-mutating requests only if safe
- review SLO/timeout/cache strategy
```

---

# 28. Runbook - Suspicious Permission Grant

## Trigger

```text id="hr1dy6"
admin assigns many roles
self-grant
permission change outside maintenance window
break-glass access
```

## Triage

```text id="kq1c7c"
1. Identify changedBy actor and auth context.
2. Verify ticket/reason/approval.
3. List old/new values.
4. Check actions performed after grant.
5. Check impersonation/delegation events.
6. Verify step-up/MFA claim if required.
```

## Containment

```text id="v80s7h"
- revoke suspicious grant
- disable admin session
- freeze permission changes for tenant if needed
- preserve audit logs
```

## Eradication

```text id="h0qfmt"
- patch admin policy
- require step-up/approval
- add alert for self-grant
- add role assignment review workflow
```

---

# 29. Runbook - Partner Replay / Invalid Signature Spike

## Triage

```text id="umowrm"
1. Identify clientId.
2. Compare invalid signature vs replay vs idempotency conflict.
3. Check recent partner deployment.
4. Check timestamp skew.
5. Inspect nonce/delivery ID duplicates.
6. Confirm no duplicate business mutation occurred.
```

## Containment

```text id="hfu6lv"
- throttle clientId
- temporarily disable client if malicious
- rotate HMAC secret if capture suspected
- enforce stricter timestamp window
```

## Recovery

```text id="y36nl8"
- coordinate with partner
- re-enable after valid signatures
- verify idempotency store
- audit impacted resources
```

---

# 30. Runbook - Confirmed Authorization Bypass

## Immediate

```text id="rzlcnm"
1. Declare incident.
2. Freeze risky endpoint or feature flag it off.
3. Revoke suspected tokens/sessions/API keys.
4. Preserve logs/audit/traces.
5. Identify affected tenants/resources.
```

## Scope

```text id="etr1f1"
- endpoint(s)
- actor(s)
- tenant(s)
- resource IDs
- time window
- data returned/modified
- permission state at time
- deployments/config changes
```

## Contain

```text id="sknf8c"
- patch policy/mapper
- block route/gateway rule
- rotate keys/secrets if credential issue
- disable compromised account/client
```

## Recover

```text id="8x7yds"
- redeploy fix
- run backfill audit query
- notify stakeholders per policy/legal
- add regression tests
- update threat model
```

---

# 31. Forensic Query - Who Accessed Resource?

```sql id="57d0wb"
SELECT
    event_time,
    event_type,
    decision,
    reason,
    actor_type,
    actor_subject_id,
    actor_tenant_id,
    action,
    resource_type,
    resource_id,
    policy_name,
    policy_version,
    correlation_id
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE resource_type = 'case'
  AND resource_id = 'CASE-789'
ORDER BY event_time DESC;
```

---

# 32. Forensic Query - What Did User Do?

```sql id="3f064r"
SELECT
    event_time,
    event_type,
    decision,
    reason,
    action,
    resource_type,
    resource_id,
    tenant_id,
    correlation_id
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE actor_subject_id = 'user-123'
  AND event_time BETWEEN :startTime AND :endTime
ORDER BY event_time;
```

---

# 33. Forensic Query - Permission Changes Before Incident

```sql id="dndnoz"
SELECT
    event_time,
    event_type,
    changed_by,
    target_subject_id,
    tenant_id,
    permission_code,
    role_code,
    old_value,
    new_value,
    reason,
    correlation_id
FROM entitlement_audit_event
WHERE target_subject_id = 'user-123'
  AND event_time BETWEEN :incidentStart - interval '7 days'
                     AND :incidentEnd
ORDER BY event_time;
```

---

# 34. Forensic Query - Cross-Tenant Attempts

```sql id="p4a1xk"
SELECT
    actor_subject_id,
    actor_tenant_id,
    target_tenant_id,
    action,
    resource_type,
    count(*) AS attempts,
    count(DISTINCT resource_id) AS distinct_resources,
    min(event_time) AS first_seen,
    max(event_time) AS last_seen
FROM security_audit_event
WHERE event_type = 'CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED'
  AND event_time BETWEEN :startTime AND :endTime
GROUP BY actor_subject_id, actor_tenant_id, target_tenant_id, action, resource_type
ORDER BY attempts DESC;
```
